+
+# [LSU][V3.2.11][A3] Only the CHAOS watchdog's registered FAIL event
+# ("m5_fail instruction encountered" -> ExitEvent.FAIL, raised by the
+# LQ04/SQ03/SP05 watchdogs via exitSimLoop(..., 1)) is propagated to the
+# process exit status. The SE guest exit syscall also carries a status
+# (exitSimLoop("exiting with last active thread context", guest_status);
+# syscall_emul.cc:250) — that is the workload's own answer, not a simulator
+# failure — so it and every other non-zero cause stay un-propagated.
+_exit_code = simulator.get_last_exit_event_code()
+if (_exit_code
+        and simulator.get_last_exit_event_cause()
+        == "m5_fail instruction encountered"):
+    sys.exit(_exit_code)

+# LQ04 Research Adjudication — v3.2.11
+
+**Date:** 2026-11-10
+**Branch / base:** `lsu-platform-v3.2.11` @ `fb94ebae` (Task 2 tip; this record is the next commit)
+**Adjudicated artifacts:** `docs/gem5-fi/lsu/implementation_v3_2/pilots_v3_2_10/LQ04-R01..R05/` (five fault pilots) and `LQ04-CONTROL-R01..R05/` (five matched no-fault controls) — read-only; no artifact under any `pilots_v3_2_*` was modified.
+**Tooling (v3.2.11, Task 2):** `platform/trace_completeness.check_completeness` (returns `structural_chain_completeness`, the structural alias of `propagation_coverage`) and `platform/fault_effect_coverage.classify` (returns `fault_effect_propagation_coverage`), wired exactly as `platform/run_pilot.py` wires them: records from the closed `trace_v31.jsonl`, injection = last `injection`/`injection_nochange` event, terminal = the trace's own summary `terminal_state`.
+**Cross-check (not source of truth):** `LQ04_Propagation_Audit_v3.2.10.md` — every number below was re-derived from the ten pilot directories in this worktree; where the v3.2.10 audit reports the same value it is noted in §7.
+**Provenance:** binary SHA `f3c2a4423aab81ca565c7adcfb840a7b8f9cdb9f2a6b6b5ecc3d0e148af20694` and workload SHA `fa25f785a4822eb578ae8da2d26c400d7450f40d975b2d7da84de900b48d97ba` (verification in §6.1; `build/ARM/gem5.opt` is absent in this fresh worktree, so the binary SHA is cited from the ten run manifests, all identical); golden oracle `checksum=0xb097eb83f71545cb` (`oracle_source=workload_golden`).
+
+---
+
+## 0. Frozen verdict (applies to the five fault pilots LQ04-R01..R05)
+
+```text
+RecordIntegrity=VALID
+TraceCompleteness=COMPLETE
+StructuralChainCompleteness=COMPLETE
+FaultEffectPropagationCoverage=LIFECYCLE_ONLY
+PROPAGATION_VERIFIED=0
+```
+
+This block is the frozen LQ04 verdict quoted by the v3.2.11 model-status matrix, the Excel
+workbook and the final report. It is the aggregate of the five fault pilots re-derived in §2;
+each of the five rows independently reproduces every line of the block. The five matched
+controls are adjudicated separately in §2 and §3.4 (their honest structural signature is
+`PARTIAL` with `missing_stages=[injection]` — there is no fault to propagate by design).
+
+## 1. What this record decides
+
+v3.2.11 splits the question the v3.2.10 label `propagation_coverage=COMPLETE` used to answer
+into two dimensions that must never be merged:
+
+- **StructuralChainCompleteness** — did the expected stage kinds appear on the fault's own
+  chain (injection → memory_response → register_writeback → consumer_commit), and are the
+  chain's value hashes continuous? This is a claim about *trace structure*.
+- **FaultEffectPropagationCoverage** — did the injected fault actually diverge a value or a
+  control decision, and did that divergence reach a committed architectural outcome? This is
+  a claim about *fault effect*.
+
+For LQ04 the two answers differ, and the difference is the research result: the chain is
+structurally complete end to end, and it transports the **correct** payload — the fault's
+effect (entry-lifetime corruption: `valid 1→0`, `request 1→0`) never enters the data stream.
+
+## 2. Per-pilot re-derivation (all ten runs)
+
+Re-derived in this worktree with the §6.2 command (real output quoted there). Terminal is the
+trace summary's own `terminal_state`, cross-checked equal to `pilot_meta.json`
+`classification.terminal_state` in all ten runs.
+
+### 2.1 Fault pilots
+
+| Run | Identity (SampleRunID) | Injections | Activation | Terminal | Oracle | RecordIntegrity | TraceCompleteness | StructuralChain | FaultEffectCoverage | Effect reason |
+|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
+| LQ04-R01 | LQ04-F0-W2-S01-R01 | 1 | TRIGGERED | MASKED | PASS | VALID | COMPLETE | COMPLETE | LIFECYCLE_ONLY | TRIGGERED_NO_DIVERGENCE |
+| LQ04-R02 | LQ04-F0-W2-S01-R02 | 1 | TRIGGERED | MASKED | PASS | VALID | COMPLETE | COMPLETE | LIFECYCLE_ONLY | TRIGGERED_NO_DIVERGENCE |
+| LQ04-R03 | LQ04-F0-W2-S01-R03 | 1 | TRIGGERED | MASKED | PASS | VALID | COMPLETE | COMPLETE | LIFECYCLE_ONLY | TRIGGERED_NO_DIVERGENCE |
+| LQ04-R04 | LQ04-F0-W2-S01-R04 | 1 | TRIGGERED | MASKED | PASS | VALID | COMPLETE | COMPLETE | LIFECYCLE_ONLY | TRIGGERED_NO_DIVERGENCE |
+| LQ04-R05 | LQ04-F0-W2-S01-R05 | 1 | TRIGGERED | MASKED | PASS | VALID | COMPLETE | COMPLETE | LIFECYCLE_ONLY | TRIGGERED_NO_DIVERGENCE |
+
+Per-run classifier statistics (§6.2 output): chain events 335/336/335/335/336;
+`value_divergence_count=0` and `control_divergence_count=0` in all five (LQ04 has no control
+markers by design — its observable effect *is* the entry-lifecycle window);
+`downstream_commit_types=[commit, consumer_commit, errant_commit]`; `missing_stages=[]`;
+`chain_integrity=PASS`; propagation stages present: `injection, memory_response,
+register_writeback, consumer_commit, errant_commit` (the profile for `(LQ04, MASKED)` is
+satisfied by its first alternative). The five injection targets are five distinct
+`(seq, pc, lq_idx, gen, addr)` tuples — ordinals 1–5 each exactly once (§6.3).
+
+### 2.2 Matched no-fault controls
+
+| Run | Identity (SampleRunID) | Injections | Activation | Terminal | Oracle | RecordIntegrity | TraceCompleteness | StructuralChain | FaultEffectCoverage | Effect reason |
+|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
+| LQ04-CONTROL-R01 | LQ04-F0-W2-S01-R01 | 0 | NOT_TRIGGERED | UNRESOLVED | PASS | VALID | COMPLETE | PARTIAL | UNRESOLVED | NO_INJECTION_EVENT |
+| LQ04-CONTROL-R02 | LQ04-F0-W2-S01-R02 | 0 | NOT_TRIGGERED | UNRESOLVED | PASS | VALID | COMPLETE | PARTIAL | UNRESOLVED | NO_INJECTION_EVENT |
+| LQ04-CONTROL-R03 | LQ04-F0-W2-S01-R03 | 0 | NOT_TRIGGERED | UNRESOLVED | PASS | VALID | COMPLETE | PARTIAL | UNRESOLVED | NO_INJECTION_EVENT |
+| LQ04-CONTROL-R04 | LQ04-F0-W2-S01-R04 | 0 | NOT_TRIGGERED | UNRESOLVED | PASS | VALID | COMPLETE | PARTIAL | UNRESOLVED | NO_INJECTION_EVENT |
+| LQ04-CONTROL-R05 | LQ04-F0-W2-S01-R05 | 0 | NOT_TRIGGERED | UNRESOLVED | PASS | VALID | COMPLETE | PARTIAL | UNRESOLVED | NO_INJECTION_EVENT |
+
+Control semantics (per the v3.2.10 audit §3, re-verified in §6.5): `lq04_injections.log`
+absent by construction (injector not mounted; `--probability 0.0`, no `--chaos_lq04`);
+`grep -c CHAOS_LQ04` on each control `stdout.log` = 0; terminal `UNRESOLVED /
+NO_INJECTION_EVENT` is the honest no-fault signature, not a failure; the identical golden
+checksum in all five controls means the fault runs' checksum is not run-to-run variance.
+
+## 3. The evidence that separates the two dimensions
+
+### 3.1 Structural chain complete (the STRUCTURAL side)
+
+For each fault pilot the chain (events sharing the injection's `ptag` or `dyn_inst_seq`)
+contains every expected stage — `injection`, `memory_response`, `register_writeback`,
+`consumer_commit` — plus `commit`, `errant_commit` and the `lq_lifecycle` records
+(alloc / response_probe / restore / free). `check_completeness` reports
+`missing_stages=[]` and `chain_integrity=PASS` (its hash-continuity checks — response
+`data_fnv` → writeback `source_value_hash`, writeback `writeback_value_fnv` → consumer
+`consumer_input_fnv` — found no discontinuity). Re-derived hash agreement per pilot (§6.4):
+
+- response `data_fnv` == writeback `writeback_value_fnv` == writeback `payload_fnv`:
+  `c6d4496e17859d5` (R01), `46f9187bab44bc35` (R02), `14fe41788a431ad5` (R03),
+  `4f8a555d540fe9f5` (R04), `f9468ffeedcb9ad5` (R05) — `True` in all five;
+- first consumer `source_value_hash` == its `consumer_input_fnv` — `True` in all five.
+
+The structural chain is therefore complete **and hash-continuous**. That is exactly what the
+historical `propagation_coverage=COMPLETE` recorded (§4).
+
+### 3.2 Fault effect confined to the entry lifecycle (the EFFECT side)
+
+The same chain, graded for effect by `fault_effect_coverage.classify`, shows **zero
+divergence** — `value_divergence_count=0`, `control_divergence_count=0`,
+`fault_effect_reason=TRIGGERED_NO_DIVERGENCE` — hence `LIFECYCLE_ONLY`, not
+`COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN`. The per-record flags behind that count (re-derived in §6.4):
+
+- the single chained `register_writeback` per pilot carries `data_corruption=0` and
+  `value_matches_payload=1` (5/5 pilots);
+- every chained `consumer_commit` carries `consumer_input_matches_source=1`:
+  326/326 (R01), 327/327 (R02), 326/326 (R03), 326/326 (R04), 327/327 (R05) —
+  `consumer_input_matches_source=0` count is **0** in all five;
+- `taint_value_mismatch_records=0` (pilot_meta, all five).
+
+So the complete, hash-continuous dataflow graph transports the **correct** payload. The
+fault's corrupting effect — `valid 1→0`, `request 1→0` at `executeLoad_post`, no pop, no
+reuse, restore at the commit boundary — lives entirely inside the LQ-entry lifecycle window
+(injection tick → restore tick), which is what `LIFECYCLE_ONLY` names.
+
+### 3.3 No response loss, correct checksum (the OUTCOME side)
+
+- Response binding (re-derived §6.6): `association=sender_state`,
+  `entry_valid_at_response=0`, `entry_request_at_response=0`, `data_unchanged=1` in all five
+  fault pilots — the in-flight response arrived while the entry was visibly invalid, was
+  recovered through `pkt->senderState`, and delivered the anchor's correct payload. No
+  response was dropped and none was mis-bound.
+- Oracle (§6.3/§6.5): every one of the ten `oracle.log` files ends `ORACLE_PASS`; the last
+  line of every one of the ten `stdout.log` files is `checksum=0xb097eb83f71545cb`, equal to
+  the golden oracle. No application SDC.
+
+### 3.4 Why the controls' `PARTIAL` is the honest value
+
+A control has no injection event, so no chain identity exists and the expected-stage profile
+for LQ04 cannot be satisfied (`missing_stages=[injection]`, `chain_integrity=UNKNOWN`,
+`fault_effect=UNRESOLVED / NO_INJECTION_EVENT`). Reporting `COMPLETE` there would assert a
+fault chain that does not exist. The control's job — reproducing the golden checksum with
+the injector absent, five times — it fulfils exactly.
+
+## 4. Historical artifacts are unchanged
+
+The ten `pilots_v3_2_10/*/pilot_meta.json` (and every other file under `pilots_v3_2_*`)
+are **not modified** by this record or by anything in the v3.2.11 round: all re-derivation
+commands are read-only, and `git status --porcelain` was clean apart from this new document
+(§6.7). The historical `propagation_coverage=COMPLETE` those files record for the five fault
+pilots (and `PARTIAL` for the five controls) is the **structural label only** — stage
+presence on the fault's own chain — produced by the pre-v3.2.11 tooling, which answered only
+the structural question. It is retained verbatim as a historical record. The research
+adjudication of fault-effect propagation is carried by this document's
+`FaultEffectPropagationCoverage=LIFECYCLE_ONLY` / `PROPAGATION_VERIFIED=0`, not by rewriting
+the pilots. The same applies to `fault_causality=DIRECT_DATAFLOW_EVIDENCE` in the fault
+pilots' `pilot_meta.json`: it is the platform's structural label for a chain with direct
+dataflow edges, and the same file's `accepted_meaning` states it is "record validity; not a
+claim that the fault caused the terminal" — under §3.2 those edges carry the correct value,
+so it must not be read as fault-effect propagation.
+
+## 5. Preserved research conclusion
+
+Unchanged from v3.2.10 and re-confirmed by this re-derivation:
+
+- **5/5 injections** — one injection per fault pilot, five distinct targets, ordinals 1–5
+  each exactly once (§6.3);
+- **5/5 oracle PASS** — and 5/5 in the controls as well (§6.3, §6.5);
+- **5/5 MASKED** — terminal `MASKED` / `TARGET_COMMITTED_DATA_VALUE_PRESENT`, target
+  committed with the correct data value present (§6.2);
+- **no response loss** — senderState binding recorded in all five (§3.3);
+- **no wrong-data propagation** — zero diverged values, zero mismatched consumer edges
+  (§3.2);
+- **no application SDC** — checksum equals the golden oracle in all ten runs (§6.4, §6.5).
+
+And the honesty boundary that motivates this record: **the fault definition was not changed
+to obtain SDC.** LQ04 remains what the v3.2.10 freeze defined — corruption of LQ-entry
+lifetime metadata (`LQEntry::_valid / _request`) after `executeLoad`, no pop, no reuse,
+restore at the commit boundary. The `LIFECYCLE_ONLY` / `PROPAGATION_VERIFIED=0` verdict is
+the truthful outcome of that definition under the implemented response path (senderState
+binding), reported as such rather than relabelled.
+

+
+**Date:** 2026-11-10
+**Audit target:** `CHAOS/gem5/src/cpu/o3/lsq_unit.cc` — the v3.2.10 LQ04
+implementation source (injector hook, side channel, boundary restore).
+**Audited at:** commit `8f32a897` on branch `lsu-platform-v3.2.11`
+(worktree `.claude/worktrees/lsu-platform-v3.2.11`), clean tree. The audited
+file was last modified by `a6abe75d` "[LSU][MDSPEC][V3.2.10][LQ04] inject at
+executeLoad with response probe and boundary restore trace" (2026-11-09) —
+i.e. the file is exactly the v3.2.10 post-implementation state and has not
+changed since.

+**Revision (2026-11-10, review-fix round at HEAD `ee1f00dc`):** §2's
+"task-6 brief expressed" column was corrected — the brief's ranges are
+return-line based, and `commitLoad` 945-973 is an exact match, not a
+deviation; §3.7 gained a one-sentence reconciliation of the window/loop

+and 11; and the header's frozen-fields pointer was fixed to §4.

+§1.4 remains valid at the revision HEAD. The seven authoritative verified

+## 2. Correction table (old Excel v3.2.5 → verified v3.2.10)
+
+"Old Excel v3.2.5" quotes cell `G4` of sheet `4.源码Hook审计` verbatim (see §6).
+"Verified v3.2.10" is the span from the return-type line through the closing
+brace at HEAD `8f32a897` (§1.4). The "task-6 brief" column reconciles the ranges
+the v3.2.11 task-6 brief expressed against this evidence. The brief's ranges are
+**return-line based** — each starts at the return-type line, exactly as the
+brief's own parentheticals state ("void at 394", "Fault at 719", "void 1202").
+
+| function | old Excel v3.2.5 (`G4`) | verified v3.2.10 (this HEAD) | task-6 brief expressed | evidence (command → line) | responsibility |
+|---|---|---|---|---|---|
+| `insertLoad` | `353-414` | **394-463** (name :395; body :397-462; `}` :463) | 394-464 — start :394 (the return-type line) is exact; the end :464 over-counts the blank separator after the closing brace :463 | `grep -n` → `395:LSQUnit::insertLoad(...)`; `awk 'NR>=462&&NR<=465'` → `463 }`, `464` blank, `465 void` | Allocates the LQ entry: `advance_tail()` :405, `back().set(load_inst)` :410, assigns `lqEntryGeneration` :411 and `lqIdx` :412, emits the `alloc`/`reuse` lifecycle record :413-419. |
+| `executeLoad` | *(absent — no line range in `G4`)* | **719-831** (name :720; body :722-830; `}` :831) | 719-831 — exact match | `grep -n` → `720:LSQUnit::executeLoad(...)`; `sed -n '830,831p'` → `return load_fault;` / `}` | Issues the memory request (`initiateAcc` :736), handles predication (:738-745, :763-778) and partial-fault/split retry (:750-759), returns the memory-order-violation fault for replay (:789-791), and **hosts the LQ04 injection hook at the function tail** (:796-828). |
+| LQ04 injection hook | *(absent)* | **796-828** (comment :796-803; injected block :804-828), inside `executeLoad` | 796-828 — exact match | `sed -n '796,804p'`; `grep -n corruptLifetime` → `:817` | After a clean `executeLoad` round, the ordinal-selected target moves `valid`/`request` into the `LQEntry` side channel (`e.corruptLifetime()` :817); inert unless `CHAOSLQ04` is mounted. |

+| `commitLoad` | `775-798` | **945-973** (name :946; body :948-972; `}` :973) | 945-973 — exact match | `grep -n` → `946:LSQUnit::commitLoad()`; `sed -n '948,950p'` → restore-before-assert | Restore-before-assert: `lq04Restore(front(), "commit")` :948-949 precedes `assert(front().valid())` :950; then frees/pops (`clear()` :969, `pop_front()` :970) after the `free`/`commit` lifecycle record :966-968. |
+| `commitLoads` | *(absent)* | **975-987** (name :976; body :978-986; `}` :987) | 975-987 — exact match | `grep -n` → `976:LSQUnit::commitLoads(...)`; `sed -n '980,981p'` | Accepts a corrupt head before the loop: `assert(size()==0 \|\| front().valid() \|\| front().lq04Corrupt())` :980-981; then the while loop :983-986 calls `commitLoad()` :985. |
+| `squash` | `986-1020` | **1202-1338** (name :1203; body :1205-1337; `}` :1338); LQ04 load-side window :1205-1254 | 1202-1253 — **truncated**: the start :1202 (the return-type line) is exact, but the end :1253 is `++stats.squashedLoads;`, the last statement of the load-side while loop (which closes at :1254); the function continues through the HTM-uid fixup (:1256-1294) and the store-side squash loop (:1296-1336), closing at :1338 | `grep -n` → `1203:LSQUnit::squash(...)`; `sed -n '1237,1238p'`; `awk 'NR>=1337&&NR<=1341'` → `1338 }`, `1340 uint64_t`, `1341 LSQUnit::getLatestHtmUid()` | Restores before clear/pop on the load side: `lq04Restore(back(), "squash")` :1237-1238 precedes `clear()` :1250 and `pop_back()` :1252; the loop never checks `valid()`. |
+
+**Reconciliation with the task-6 brief.** The brief expresses its ranges from
+the return-type line (e.g. `insertLoad` "394-464 (void at 394; name 395; body
+397-463)"). Five of the seven match the verified evidence exactly:
+`executeLoad` 719-831, hook 796-828, `lq04Restore` 927-943, `commitLoad`
+945-973, `commitLoads` 975-987. One includes the blank separator line after
+the closing brace instead of stopping at the brace (`insertLoad` 394-464 vs
+`}` :463 — the start :394 is exact), a one-line over-count at the end only.
+One is truncated (`squash` 1202-1253 vs `}` :1338 — the start :1202 is
+exact). The authoritative values for Task 7 to transcribe are the
+grep-verified spans in the "verified v3.2.10" column; the brief's own
+parenthetical boundary data (void :394 / name :395 / body 397-463 for
+`insertLoad`, etc.) is confirmed by §1.3 except for the `squash` body end
+(:1253 is not the function end — the load-side loop closes at :1254 and the
+function at :1338).

+
+## 3. Per-function evidence and responsibilities
+
+### 3.1 `LSQUnit::insertLoad` — `lsq_unit.cc:394-463`
+
+Signature (`sed -n '394,396p'`):
+
+```cpp
+void
+LSQUnit::insertLoad(const DynInstPtr &load_inst)
+{
+```
+
+Responsibility: allocate the LQ entry for a dispatched load. Verified key
+lines: `loadQueue.advance_tail()` **:405** (grows the circular queue);
+`loadQueue.back().set(load_inst)` **:410** (writes `_valid = true`);
+`load_inst->lqEntryGeneration = ++lqEntryGeneration;` **:411** (the generation
+counter is an `LSQUnit` member, the value is stored on the DynInst — see
+`LQ04_Source_Audit_v3.2.10.md` §1.2); `load_inst->lqIdx = loadQueue.tail();`
+**:412**; and the pre-existing CHAOS lifecycle hook
+`noteLQLifecycle(..., reused ? "reuse" : "alloc", "insert")` **:413-419**
+(including the `lq_last_alloc` bookkeeping that classifies alloc vs reuse).
+The LQ04 fault never touches this function: it does not pop, `set()` or free
+the slot, so `assert(!loadQueue.back().valid())` (:409) stays satisfied.
+
+### 3.2 `LSQUnit::executeLoad` — `lsq_unit.cc:719-831`
+
+Signature (`sed -n '719,721p'`):
+
+```cpp
+Fault
+LSQUnit::executeLoad(const DynInstPtr &inst)
+{
+```
+
+Responsibility: execute one load issue round. Verified key lines:
+`++inst->executionAttemptId;` **:723**; `load_fault = inst->initiateAcc();`
+**:736** (issues the request — the `LSQ::pushRequest` chain installs it on the
+entry via `LSQRequest::install`); predication handling
+`if (load_fault == NoFault && !inst->readMemAccPredicate())` **:738-745** and
+the fault/predicated-false send-to-commit path
+`if (load_fault != NoFault || !inst->readPredicate())` **:763-778**;
+partial-fault split retry **:750-759**; memory-order violation check
+`Fault viol_fault = checkViolations(it, inst);` **:789** with
+`return viol_fault;` **:790-791** (a violation returns immediately — the load
+is replayed; a clean check falls through to the injection point). The LQ04
+injection hook is hosted at the function tail, **:796-828**, immediately before
+`return load_fault;` **:830**.
+
+### 3.3 LQ04 injection hook — `lsq_unit.cc:796-828` (inside `executeLoad`)
+
+Comment block **:796-803** (quoted in §1.3) and injected block **:804-828**:
+
+```cpp
+    if (cpu->chaosLQ04 && load_fault == NoFault && inst->isLoad()) {
+        LQEntry &e = loadQueue[inst->lqIdx];
+        if (chaos_lq04::isEligible(e.valid(), e.hasRequest(),
+                                   inst->isSquashed(),
+                                   inst->isExecuted())) {
+            ...
+                e.corruptLifetime();        // :817
+            ...
+        }
+    }                                       // :828
+```
+
+Responsibility: the ordinal-selected target entry moves `valid`/`request` into
+the `LQEntry` side channel (visibly `valid=0`, `request=nullptr`) after a
+successful `executeLoad` round, until its commit/squash boundary. No pop, no
+early free/reuse; the returned fault, the response binding (`pkt->senderState`)
+and the data are untouched. The block is inert unless `CHAOSLQ04` is mounted.
+
+### 3.4 `LSQUnit::lq04Restore` — `lsq_unit.cc:927-943`
+
+Signature (`sed -n '927,929p'`):
+
+```cpp
+void
+LSQUnit::lq04Restore(LQEntry& entry, const char* reason)
+{
+```
+
+Responsibility: restore a corrupted entry's lifetime at its commit/squash
+boundary. Preceded by the header comment **:923-926** ("The stashed request
+moves back to `_request` and the entry becomes valid again, so the normal
+`clear()`/`freeLSQEntry()` path runs unchanged. No-op for entries that were
+never corrupted."). Body: `noteRestore` **:934-936** and
+`noteLQLifecycle(..., "restore", reason)` **:937-941** emit the restore trace;
+`entry.restoreLifetime();` **:942** moves the stashed request/valid back into
+the entry. This function did not exist in the pre-implementation source, so the
+v3.2.5 row has no old value for it.
+
+### 3.5 `LSQUnit::commitLoad` — `lsq_unit.cc:945-973`
+
+Signature (`sed -n '945,947p'`):
+
+```cpp
+void
+LSQUnit::commitLoad()
+{
+```
+
+Responsibility: retire the head load. The LQ04-relevant ordering is
+restore-before-assert (**:948-950**):
+
+```cpp
+    if (loadQueue.front().lq04Corrupt())
+        lq04Restore(loadQueue.front(), "commit");
+    assert(loadQueue.front().valid());
+```
+
+then the free/pop: `noteLQLifecycle(..., "free", "commit")` **:966-968**,
+`loadQueue.front().clear()` **:969** (frees the request via `freeLSQEntry()`
+and writes `_valid = false`), `loadQueue.pop_front()` **:970**.
+
+### 3.6 `LSQUnit::commitLoads` — `lsq_unit.cc:975-987`
+
+Signature (`sed -n '975,977p'`):
+
+```cpp
+void
+LSQUnit::commitLoads(InstSeqNum &youngest_inst)
+{
+```
+
+Responsibility: commit head loads up to `youngest_inst`. Before the loop it
+accepts a still-corrupt head (**:978-981**):
+
+```cpp
+    // LQ04: the head may still be visibly invalid when the fault window has
+    // not closed yet; commitLoad() restores it before its own valid() check.
+    assert(loadQueue.size() == 0 || loadQueue.front().valid()
+            || loadQueue.front().lq04Corrupt());
+```
+
+The loop itself (**:983-986**) calls `commitLoad()` (**:985**), which restores
+before its own `valid()` assert (§3.5).
+
+### 3.7 `LSQUnit::squash` — `lsq_unit.cc:1202-1338`
+
+Signature (`sed -n '1202,1204p'`):
+
+```cpp
+void
+LSQUnit::squash(const InstSeqNum &squashed_num)
+{
+```
+
+Responsibility: squashes younger loads and stores on a misprediction/flush.
+The LQ04-relevant load side is the first while loop (**:1209-1254**), which
+tests only `seqNum > squashed_num` and **never checks `valid()`**; per entry it
+restores before clear/pop (**:1237-1238**, **:1250**, **:1252**):
+
+```cpp
+        if (loadQueue.back().lq04Corrupt())
+            lq04Restore(loadQueue.back(), "squash");
+        ...
+        loadQueue.back().clear();
+        ...
+        loadQueue.pop_back();
+        ++stats.squashedLoads;              // :1253 — loop tail, NOT function end
+    }                                       // :1254 — closes the load while loop
+```
+
+The function then continues past :1254: `lqAvgOccupancy` update **:1256**, the
+HTM in-flight-uid fixup **:1258-1294**, and the store-side squash while loop
+**:1296-1336** (store `clear()` :1332, `pop_back()` :1334,
+`++stats.squashedStores` :1335), closing with
+`stats.sqAvgOccupancy = queueOccupancy(storeQueue);` **:1337** and the function
+brace **:1338**. Any audit range that ends at :1253 or :1254 truncates the
+function; the LQ04-relevant window within it is :1205-1254. (The two labels
+used in this document are consistent: the **window** :1205-1254 starts at the
+function body's first statement — the squash `DPRINTF` of :1205-1207 — and
+runs through the load while loop's closing brace at :1254, while :1209-1254
+is the **while loop proper**; :1205-1208 is the loop's preamble.)
+

+## 3.1 Model-specific evidence quote

+`old_addr=7ffffefda0;new_addr=7ffffefda8` (v3.2.2 R01); stderr `Page table fault when accessing virtual address 0` (injected EA 0x7ffffefda8 — no match).
+
+## 4. Terminal and causality
+
+The EA transform and its downstream request/response/writeback/consumer are present. The audit cannot prove the delivered value is wrong: the trace records no reference value for the correct EA. The two crash runs do not establish fault causality — A01 v3.2.2 R01 panicked on VA 0 while the injected EA was 0x7ffffefda8, and the v3.2.1 stderr contains no page-table-fault address. v3.2.1 has no oracle.log.
+
+## 5. Missing evidence
+
+NO_REFERENCE_VALUE_AT_CORRECT_EA; CRASH_ADDRESS_MISMATCH; ORACLE_FILE_MISSING (v3.2.1); truncated v3.2.1 traces lack commit/consumer in R03
+
+## 6. Verdict
+
+- PARTIAL: 7/7 pilots
+
+Model verdict: **PARTIAL** (see per-pilot split above).
+
+## 7. Supplementary pilot recommendation
+
+Optional: add the correct-EA reference data hash and the page-table-fault address to the A01 trace, then 1-2 supplementary pilots. Not needed for the current verdict; awaiting approval.

+## 3.1 Model-specific evidence quote
+
+`size=4;old_be=0xf;new_be=0xe;store_data_hex=c114af81`; store_mem_write `be_mask=0xe;lane0_packet=0xc1;lane0_before=0xa5;lane0_after=0xa5`; later load response `a514af81`; writeback `81af14a5`.
+
+## 4. Terminal and causality
+
+v3.2.6 R04/R05 are complete causal chains. R04: be 0xF→0xE, store writes c114af81 with lane0 disabled; store_mem_write records lane0_before=a5/lane0_after=a5 (disabled_unchanged=1); a later load returns a514af81, writes back 81af14a5, and its consumer receives 0x81af14a5; the E2E evidence records 1009 consumers and oracle FAIL (SDC). R05 is the same pattern with 7714 consumers. The raw oracle.log/stdout for these two runs is absent from the v3.2.6 directory; the oracle FAIL is recorded in pilot_meta and DA04_v326_E2E_EVIDENCE.txt. v3.2.4 R01 is SQUASHED (PARTIAL); R02/R03 read no lane (LIFECYCLE_ONLY); v3.2.4 R04/R05 carry an SDC terminal but no chain in the older trace.
+
+## 5. Missing evidence
+
+ORACLE_RAW_FILE_MISSING for v3.2.6 R04/R05 (meta/E2E only); v3.2.4 R04/R05 chain absent (superseded by v3.2.6)
+
+## 6. Verdict
+
+- COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN: 2/7 pilots
+- LIFECYCLE_ONLY: 2/7 pilots
+- PARTIAL: 3/7 pilots
+
+Model verdict: **PARTIAL** (see per-pilot split above).
+
+## 7. Supplementary pilot recommendation
+
+Recover or regenerate the raw oracle.log/stdout for the two v3.2.6 runs (2 runs, preserved identities) if a raw application-layer checksum is required. The microarchitectural chain is already complete.

+# LSU v3.2.11 — Task 9: archived raw-trace extraction and hash verification (B2)
+
+**Extraction date:** 2026-11-10
+**Worktree:** `/home/sdc/gem5-fi-ding/.claude/worktrees/lsu-platform-v3.2.11`, branch `lsu-platform-v3.2.11`, HEAD `776558fc6b6fbdc5d6e7921360e22fea76d5ca82`
+**Scope (task brief):** extract only the pilot evidence sets whose raw `trace_v31.jsonl` is not in-tree — SQ01 (v3.2.3 archive), FW05 (v3.2.7), SQ03 (v3.2.8), SP05 (v3.2.9) — into `audit_v3_2_11/extracted_evidence/` (gitignored scratch), write a `.sha256` sidecar next to every extracted trace, verify each hash against the recorded checksum sources, and prove the historical `pilots_v3_2_*` trees were untouched. No file under any `pilots_v3_2_*` was read-write; every archive/pilot access was read-only.
+
+## 1. Source archives
+

+A fifth archive was located and hashed but deliberately **not** extracted (out of Task 9 scope): `/home/sdc/gem5-fi-ding/.claude/worktrees/lsu-platform-v3.2/LSU_v3.2.4_DA04_Implementation_Package.zip`, SHA256 `8413a130ad76d7d894c47886fe806a114c450f02b0db5b3ff423875cb7f6fdc3`, 43234587 bytes — it contains the **v3.2.4 SQ01 re-run** traces at `docs/gem5-fi/lsu/implementation_v3_2/pilots_v3_2_4/SQ01-R0*/trace_v31.jsonl` (~197.7 MB each). Pointer recorded for the SQ01 audit task (§9).
+
+## 2. What was extracted (and how)
+
+Per pilot, exactly these 10 files: `trace_v31.jsonl`, `pilot_meta.json`, `manifest.json`, `oracle.log`, `stdout.log`, `stderr.log`, `config.json`, `config.ini`, `stats.txt`, `lsq_fwd_injections.log` (the injection log for all four models). Deliberately not extracted: `prop_trace.csv.gz` (compressed raw CSV, not in the task's file list), `citations.bib`, `command.txt` — they remain in the archives.
+
+Extraction used `unzip -j` (junk paths) with an explicit per-pilot, per-file argument list, targeting `extracted_evidence/<MODEL>_v3_2_<X>/<MODEL>-R0Y/`. `-j` is mandatory for the v3.2.7 zip, whose members carry repo-shaped paths (`docs/gem5-fi/lsu/implementation_v3_2/pilots_v3_2_7/FW05-R01/...`); without it the extraction would write into the historical `pilots_v3_2_7/` directory, which is forbidden.
+
+Exact script run (complete content of `/tmp/task9_extract.sh`, verbatim):
+
+```bash
+#!/bin/bash
+# Task 9 evidence extraction — explicit per-pilot, per-file lists only.
+set -u
+WT=/home/sdc/gem5-fi-ding/.claude/worktrees/lsu-platform-v3.2.11
+EV=$WT/docs/gem5-fi/lsu/implementation_v3_2/audit_v3_2_11/extracted_evidence
+Z3=/home/sdc/gem5-fi-ding/.claude/worktrees/lsu-platform-v3.2/LSU_v3.2.3_Propagation_Closure_Package.zip
+Z7=/home/sdc/gem5-fi-ding/.claude/worktrees/lsu-platform-v3.2.10/LSU_v3.2.7_MD04_Config_Freeze_FW05_Implementation_Package.zip
+Z8=/tmp/LSU_v3.2.8_FW05_ConfigFreeze_SQ03_Pilot_Package.zip
+Z9=/tmp/LSU_v3.2.9_SP05_Implementation_SQ03_Propagation_FW05_Closure_Package.zip
+FILES="trace_v31.jsonl pilot_meta.json manifest.json oracle.log stdout.log stderr.log config.json config.ini stats.txt lsq_fwd_injections.log"
+
+declare -A PREFIX=(
+  [SQ01]="package_staging_v323/evidence/pilots_v3_2_3"
+  [FW05]="docs/gem5-fi/lsu/implementation_v3_2/pilots_v3_2_7"
+  [SQ03]="LSU_v3.2.8_PACKAGE/B_SQ03/pilots"
+  [SP05]="LSU_v3.2.9_PACKAGE/A_SP05/pilots"
+)
+declare -A ZIP=( [SQ01]=$Z3 [FW05]=$Z7 [SQ03]=$Z8 [SP05]=$Z9 )
+declare -A VINT=( [SQ01]=v3_2_3 [FW05]=v3_2_7 [SQ03]=v3_2_8 [SP05]=v3_2_9 )
+
+mkdir -p "$EV"
+for m in SQ01 FW05 SQ03 SP05; do
+  for r in 01 02 03 04 05; do
+    p=${PREFIX[$m]}/${m}-R$r
+    d=$EV/${m}_${VINT[$m]}/${m}-R$r
+    mkdir -p "$d"
+    for f in $FILES; do
+      out=$(unzip -j -o -q "${ZIP[$m]}" "$p/$f" -d "$d" 2>&1); rc=$?
+      if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
+        echo "MISSING rc=$rc ${m}-R$r/$f :: $out"
+      fi
+    done
+    echo "DONE ${m}-R$r ($(ls "$d" | wc -l) files)"
+  done
+done
+```
+
+Result (complete real output — 20 `DONE` lines, zero `MISSING` lines, i.e. all 200 requested file extractions returned exit 0):
+
+```
+DONE SQ01-R01 (10 files)
+DONE SQ01-R02 (10 files)
+DONE SQ01-R03 (10 files)
+DONE SQ01-R04 (10 files)
+DONE SQ01-R05 (10 files)
+DONE FW05-R01 (10 files)
+DONE FW05-R02 (10 files)
+DONE FW05-R03 (10 files)
+DONE FW05-R04 (10 files)
+DONE FW05-R05 (10 files)
+DONE SQ03-R01 (10 files)
+DONE SQ03-R02 (10 files)
+DONE SQ03-R03 (10 files)
+DONE SQ03-R04 (10 files)
+DONE SQ03-R05 (10 files)
+DONE SP05-R01 (10 files)
+DONE SP05-R02 (10 files)
+DONE SP05-R03 (10 files)
+DONE SP05-R04 (10 files)
+DONE SP05-R05 (10 files)
+```
+
+Extracted volume: 2.5 GiB total — `SQ01_v3_2_3` 946M, `FW05_v3_2_7` 186M, `SQ03_v3_2_8` 941M, `SP05_v3_2_9` 445M (50 files each: 5 pilots x 10 files). The directory is gitignored via the new `.gitignore` entry `docs/gem5-fi/lsu/implementation_v3_2/audit_v3_2_11/extracted_evidence/` (appended; no existing entry modified; verified with `git check-ignore -v`).
+
+## 3. Sidecars
+
+Every extracted pilot dir now carries `trace_v31.jsonl.sha256` written as `sha256sum trace_v31.jsonl` run inside that dir — format `<sha256>  trace_v31.jsonl` (two spaces, bare filename), matching the v3.2.4 sibling sidecar convention and the input the Task 8 audit tool's `HASH_MISMATCH` check reads. Example (`SQ01_v3_2_3/SQ01-R01/trace_v31.jsonl.sha256`):
+
+```
+8a251073b2ea3c1d26a06ec60ee2a482c2d8fce3062168f51a86cda195737934  trace_v31.jsonl
+```
+
+## 4. Hash verification against the recorded checksum sources
+
+Method: compute SHA256 of each extracted `trace_v31.jsonl`, then compare with every recorded source for that pilot. 40 comparisons total: **35 MATCH, 5 MISMATCH**. Every mismatch is the v3.2.4 sibling source (§4.2) — a different run set, not a corruption of the extracted bytes. **No extracted pilot is `missing=HASH_MISMATCH`** against the recorded source of its own vintage.
+
+### 4.1 SQ01 — v3.2.3 archive bytes
+
+Repo `pilots_v3_2_3/TRACE_STORAGE.md` + per-pilot sidecars `pilots_v3_2_3/SQ01-R0*/trace_v31.jsonl.sha256` (identical values inside the v3.2.3 zip, both as per-pilot sidecars and in `package_staging_v323/SHA256SUMS.txt`):

+### 4.2 SQ01 v3.2.4 siblings — expected divergence, documented
+
+The five MISMATCH rows are the `pilots_v3_2_4/SQ01-R0*/trace_v31.jsonl.sha256` sidecars. These record the **v3.2.4 re-runs** (same seeds — e.g. R01 seed 591611458 in both vintages' `pilot_meta.json` — re-executed after the v3.2.4-round SQ01 fix, hence different trace bytes, ~197.7 MB vs 198 MB), not the v3.2.3 archive contents. The v3.2.4 raw traces are not in-tree and not in the v3.2.3 package; they live in `LSU_v3.2.4_DA04_Implementation_Package.zip` (§1). This is provenance divergence between two run sets, not a hash failure of either: each vintage's bytes match that vintage's recorded sources exactly. The per-model SQ01 audit (Tasks 10-20) must state which run set it audits and must not mix them.
+

+```
+
+## 6. Missing files
+
+**None.** All 20 pilots x 10 required files were present in their archives and extracted (200/200, zero unzip failures). Two honest clarifications:
+
+- `oracle.log` **is present in all four archived vintages** (59 bytes each, e.g. `expected=ba3b3185e541ad03 got=ba3b3185e541ad03` / `ORACLE_PASS`). The known `ORACLE_FILE_MISSING` gap affects the in-tree v3.2.1-v3.2.6 pilots (kept only in `pilot_meta`), not these four archives.
+- The in-tree dirs are thinner than the archives: `pilots_v3_2_3/SQ01-R01` and `pilots_v3_2_8/SQ03-R01` track everything except `trace_v31.jsonl` (>100 MB, GitHub limit; sidecar only), while `pilots_v3_2_7/FW05-R01` additionally lacks `oracle.log`, `stderr.log` and `lsq_fwd_injections.log` (gitignored `*.log`, never force-added), and SP05 has no in-tree pilot dir at all. The archives are therefore the only source for those files — which is why this extraction includes them.
+
+## 7. History untouched (before/after proof)
+

+The LQ04-R01 hash equals the recorded value in `DELIVERABLES_SHA256_v3210.txt` line 51 (`d136131613fb009392f3f5829f370ce2ee415b442227439d6f8149597af99425  13_five_pilots/LQ04-R01/trace_v31.jsonl`, the v3.2.10 package manifest for the same trace bytes) and equals `sha256sum` of the git blob at HEAD (`git show HEAD:.../LQ04-R01/trace_v31.jsonl | sha256sum` → same value). After extraction both checks were re-run with identical results, and the repo-wide `git status --short` showed only ` M .gitignore`.

+## 8. Task 8 audit tool runs on the extracted evidence (one pilot per model)
+
+Command pattern (run from the worktree root; `--binary-sha` is each pilot's `manifest.json`-recorded binary SHA256, exercising the B9 match path; wrapped in `/usr/bin/time -v`-style resource measurement via `-f`):
+
+```bash
+/usr/bin/time -f "  elapsed=%es maxrss=%MkB" python3 tools/lsu_propagation_audit.py \
+  --model <M> \
+  --pilot-dir docs/gem5-fi/lsu/implementation_v3_2/audit_v3_2_11/extracted_evidence/<M>_v3_2_X/<M>-R01 \
+  --binary-sha <manifest-recorded SHA> --json /tmp/task9_audit_<M>.json
+```

+Key facts from the four runs:
+
+- **No extraction-defect codes**: zero `HASH_MISMATCH` (sidecar == computed hash), zero `BINARY_SHA_MISMATCH` / `BINARY_SHA_UNRECORDED` (manifest-recorded binary SHAs matched), zero `ORACLE_FILE_MISSING` / `ORACLE_META_INCONSISTENT` (`oracle.log` present and agreeing with `pilot_meta.oracle_pass` in all four).
+- The missing-evidence codes that do appear are chain-stage gaps (`NO_EARLY_ANOMALY_EVENT`, `NO_INTERMEDIATE_PROPAGATION_EVENT`, `NO_REGISTER_WRITEBACK_EVENT`, `NO_CONSUMER_EDGE`, `NO_COMMIT_EVENT`) — model semantics for the per-model audit tasks, not evidence-integrity defects. All four R01 pilots classify `LIFECYCLE_ONLY` with `StructuralChainCompleteness=COMPLETE(1/1)` and a verified oracle (e.g. SQ01-R01 expected `ba3b3185e541ad03` == actual, pass=true; FW05-R01 adjudicated SQUASHED / `TARGET_SQUASHED_NO_COMMIT` vs observed MASKED).
+- Streaming confirmed on the extracted evidence: peak RSS ~19.8 MB on the 198 MB SQ01-R01 and 197 MB SQ03-R01 traces (8.2 s / 8.1 s elapsed).
+
+## 9. Notes for the downstream tasks (10-20, 21, 22/B8)
+
+1. **SQ01 has two run sets.** The v3.2.3 bytes (extracted here, hash-verified) and the v3.2.4 re-runs (archive located in §1, not extracted). The SQ01 audit must pick its set explicitly and cite the corresponding sidecars.
+2. **SP05's `SP05_v329_EVIDENCE.csv` has no trace-hash column**; the v3.2.9 zip's `SHA256SUMS` is the recorded checksum source used (all MATCH).
+3. `extracted_evidence/` is regenerable scratch: the archives' SHA256s (§1) plus the commands in §2/§3 reproduce it byte-identically; the committed evidence is this index, never the extracted bytes.
+4. FW05-R01's audit adjudication (SQUASHED vs observed MASKED) and the stage-gap codes per model are inputs for the per-model reports and the B8 supplementary-pilot decision — not to be re-litigated in this extraction record.

+## 4. Terminal and causality
+
+FW02-R04 (v3.2.1) has the complete control chain: forwarding_decision decision=suppressed, normal_decision=forwarded, fault_decision=suppressed; the load went to memory (memory_path=1), wrote back, and its consumer committed. The data is identical (store data 0, memory data 0), so this is a control-path-only complete chain ending MASKED. The other four pilots either lack consumer/commit events or end SQUASHED without proof that the squash is fault-caused; they are PARTIAL, not failures.
+
+## 5. Missing evidence
+
+NO_CONSUMER_EDGE/NO_COMMIT_EVENT in 4/5; squash causality unproven in R01/R05; the v3.2.4 traces lack the consumer record
+
+## 6. Verdict
+
+- COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN: 1/5 pilots
+- PARTIAL: 4/5 pilots
+
+Model verdict: **PARTIAL** (see per-pilot split above).
+
+## 7. Supplementary pilot recommendation
+
+Re-run the v3.2.4 R01/R05 pair with the consumer/commit trace hook to complete the 5-pilot set; 2 runs, preserved identities. Approval required.

+## 3.1 Model-specific evidence quote
+

+
+## 4. Terminal and causality
+
+All five pilots carry the model-specific divergence record: orig_data_fnv differs from inj_data_fnv (byte0 0x1→0x9, 0x10→0x18, 0x17→0x1f, etc.), the response carries the injected data, the writeback and the first consumer receive the injected value, and the consumer input hash equals the injected payload hash. The application checksum is unchanged (MASKED) because the corrupted byte is not used in the final result. This is a complete causal chain ending in MASKED. The generic payload-vs-writeback check alone would under-report it; the audit uses the injection record.
+

+# LSU 11-Model Propagation Audit — Summary (v3.2.11)
+
+**Date:** 2026-11-10
+**Scope:** A01, A03, FW02, SQ01, DA04, LP01, MD04, FW05, SQ03, SP05, LQ04 (RP01 excluded; not implemented)
+**Evidence:** raw `trace_v31.jsonl`, `pilot_meta.json`, `manifest.json`, `oracle.log`/stdout, injection logs, archived package traces (SQ01/FW05/SQ03/SP05), plus the model-specific injection records. Historical reports were used only as cross-checks; every number below was re-derived from the artifacts in this round.
+**Method:** `tools/lsu_propagation_audit.py` (streaming seven-stage extraction) plus a manual re-inspection of each model's injection/writeback/consumer records. The generic value-vs-payload check is necessary but not sufficient: LP01 and FW05 carry their fault-effect divergence in model-specific fields (`orig_data_*` vs `inj_data_*`, `true_data_fnv` vs `alt_data_fnv`). Those records were used explicitly and are quoted in each model report.
+
+## 1. Headline answer
+
+- **Four models have a verified fault-effect propagation chain: DA04, LP01, MD04, FW05.** A fifth, FW02, has one verified control-path chain (the suppressed forwarding changed the execution path but delivered identical data).
+- **14 unique fault pilots reach `COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN` with a data-effect divergence** (DA04 2, LP01 5, MD04 3, FW05 4). Adding FW02-R04's control-path chain gives **15/55 unique pilots**.
+- **Terminal split among the 14 data-effect pilots: SDC 6, MASKED 8, SQUASHED 0, CRASH 0.** Including FW02-R04: SDC 6, MASKED 9.
+- **Seven models do not have a verified complete fault-effect chain**: A01 and A03 are `PARTIAL` (wrong EA reaches the response, but no reference-value comparison and no address-matched crash); SQ01, SQ03, SP05 and LQ04 are `LIFECYCLE_ONLY` (no downstream consumer of the corrupted state); FW02 is mixed (1 complete control-path pilot, 4 partial).
+- **`PROPAGATION_VERIFIED=1` only for DA04, LP01, MD04, FW05.** LQ04 remains 0. A pilot run alone (IMPLEMENTED/COMPILED/UNIT_TESTED/PILOT_RUN) is never treated as propagation evidence.
+
+## 2. Per-model verdict
+
+| Model | Unit | Pilot count | Complete causal chain | Lifecycle only | Partial | Model verdict | Propagated? |
+|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
+| A01 | AGU | 7 (5 v3.2.1 + 2 v3.2.2) | 0 | 0 | 7 | PARTIAL | no |
+| A03 | AGU | 6 (5 v3.2.1 + 1 v3.2.2) | 0 | 0 | 6 | PARTIAL | no |
+| FW02 | FWD | 5 | 1 (control path) | 0 | 4 | COMPLETE 1/5; PARTIAL 4/5 | pilot only |
+| SQ01 | SQ | 5 | 0 | 5 | 0 | LIFECYCLE_ONLY | no |
+| DA04 | SQ | 7 (2 v3.2.6 + 5 v3.2.4) | 2 | 2 | 3 | COMPLETE 2/5 unique | **yes** |
+| LP01 | L1D | 5 | 5 | 0 | 0 | COMPLETE 5/5 | **yes** |
+| MD04 | LSU | 5 | 3 | 0 | 2 | COMPLETE 3/5 | **yes** |
+| FW05 | FWD | 5 | 4 | 0 | 1 | COMPLETE 4/5 | **yes** |
+| SQ03 | SQ | 5 | 0 | 5 | 0 | LIFECYCLE_ONLY | no |
+| SP05 | SQ | 5 | 0 | 5 | 0 | LIFECYCLE_ONLY | no |
+| LQ04 | LQ | 5 | 0 | 5 | 0 | LIFECYCLE_ONLY | no (stays 0) |
+
+## 3. Verified chains — the decisive evidence
+
+### DA04 (byte-enable) — 2/5 complete, SDC
+- **R04:** `old_be=0xf;new_be=0xe;store_data_hex=c114af81`; `store_mem_write` records `be_mask=0xe;lane0_packet=0xc1;lane0_before=0xa5;lane0_after=0xa5` (the disabled lane is not written); a later load returns `a514af81` (lane0 = stale `a5`), writes back `81af14a5`, and its consumer receives `0x81af14a5`; `DA04_v326_E2E_EVIDENCE.txt` records 1009 consumers, 0 mismatches, `terminal=SDC reason=ORACLE_FAIL oracle=FAIL`.
+- **R05:** same structure with 7714 consumers. **Caveat:** the raw `oracle.log`/`stdout.log` for the v3.2.6 R04/R05 directories is absent; the oracle FAIL is recorded in `pilot_meta.json` and the E2E evidence file. `OracleVerified` is therefore `missing` at the raw-file level.
+- R02/R03: the lane is never read (`STORE_COMMITTED_LANE_NOT_READ`) → `LIFECYCLE_ONLY`. R01: SQUASHED → `PARTIAL`. The older v3.2.4 R04/R05 carry an SDC terminal without a chain in that trace → `PARTIAL`; the v3.2.6 re-runs are the authoritative chain.
+
+### LP01 (load response byte0) — 5/5 complete, MASKED
+- Injection record (R01): `orig_data_hex=0100000000000000;inj_data_hex=0900000000000000;byte0_before=0x1;byte0_after=0x9;mask=0x8`; the response carries the injected data, the writeback value hash equals the injected payload hash, and the first consumer's input hash equals it (`consumer_input_matches_source=1`). R03 `0x10→0x18`, R05 `0x17→0x1f`.
+- All five pilots commit with the correct application checksum (MASKED): the corrupted byte is not used in the final result. The chain is complete to the consumer; the application-level masking is explained.
+- The generic payload-vs-writeback check alone would call this `LIFECYCLE_ONLY`; the model-specific `orig_*`/`inj_*` record is the decisive evidence. Raw `oracle.log` is absent in the v3.2.6 directories; the oracle PASS is meta-recorded.
+
+### MD04 (violation-report suppression) — 3/5 complete, MASKED
+- Injection record: `family=md04;load_seq=2251;store_seq=2239;addr=0x493990;violation_report_suppressed=1;load_complete=0`. The load nevertheless executes, receives data, writes back `0`, and commits; its consumers receive the correct data and the oracle PASSes (MASKED). The verified effect is the suppressed violation report, not a corrupted value.
+- R01/R04 are SQUASHED; the trace does not prove the squash is fault-caused → `PARTIAL`.
+
+### FW05 (wrong forwarding source) — 4/5 complete, SDC
+- Injection record (R02): `true_store_seq=12155;alt_store_seq=12154;true_data_fnv=12446998b6871244;alt_data_fnv=a06ea71be9c5aa88;selected_source=alt_store`. The writeback and first consumer carry the alt data hash; the oracle prints `got=549c9e0347adaf23` (R03/f25, R04/f27, R05/f19) against golden `67afad307642983e` — SDC.
+- R01 is SQUASHED before a consumer → `PARTIAL`.
+
+### FW02 (suppressed forwarding) — 1/5 complete (control path), MASKED
+- R04 (v3.2.1): `load_seq=397;store_seq=383;decision=suppressed;normal_decision=forwarded;fault_decision=suppressed;memory_path=1`. The load took the memory path, wrote back, and its consumer committed. The data is identical (store data 0, memory data 0), so this is a control-path chain, not a data-corruption chain.
+- R01/R05: SQUASHED without proof that the squash is fault-caused; the v3.2.4 R01/R05 traces lack a consumer/commit record → `PARTIAL`.
+
+## 4. Why the other models are not verified
+
+- **A01 / A03 (AGU):** the wrong effective address is recorded and reaches the request/response; the consumer receives data from the wrong address. However, the trace has no reference value for the correct address, so it cannot prove the delivered value differs, and the crashes are not attributable: A01 v3.2.2 R01 panicked on VA `0` with injected EA `0x7ffffefda8`; A03 v3.2.2 R01 panicked at `0xffffffffffb73c50` with injected EA `0x7ffffefdb0`; the v3.2.1 stderr files have no fault address. Verdict `PARTIAL`, not `COMPLETE` and not `CRASH`.
+- **SQ01 / SQ03 / SP05:** the corrupted store/lane/completion state is written, but no later load consumes the lane and no architectural consumer exists. `STORE_COMMITTED_LANE_NOT_READ` in all pilots. Verdict `LIFECYCLE_ONLY`.
+- **LQ04:** structural chain complete, correct payload, no corruption propagation. Verdict `LIFECYCLE_ONLY`, `PROPAGATION_VERIFIED=0` (frozen).
+
+## 5. Missing evidence (per model)
+
+| Model | Missing |
+|---|---|
+| A01 | Reference value at the correct EA; address-matched crash evidence; raw oracle files for v3.2.1; truncated v3.2.1 traces lack commit/consumer in R03 |
+| A03 | Writeback/consumer events in 5/6 pilots; address-matched crash; raw oracle files for v3.2.1 |
+| FW02 | Consumer/commit in 4/5 pilots; proof that the R01/R05 squash is fault-caused |
+| SQ01 | A later load of the corrupted lane (no consumer) |
+| DA04 | Raw oracle.log/stdout for v3.2.6 R04/R05 (meta/E2E only); the v3.2.4 R04/R05 chain is absent |
+| LP01 | Raw oracle.log in the v3.2.6 directories (meta-sourced PASS) |
+| MD04 | Raw oracle.log in v3.2.6 (meta-sourced PASS); squash causality for R01/R04 |
+| FW05 | Proof that the R01 squash is fault-caused |
+| SQ03 | A later load of the lane (no consumer) |
+| SP05 | Entry-free/drain event; consumer |
+| LQ04 | none for the lifecycle verdict |
+
+## 6. Methodological finding
+
+The generic structural/effect classifier is deliberately conservative and uses the delivered payload as its reference. That is correct for LQ04 (correct payload) but **under-reports LP01 and FW05**, whose fault effect is encoded in model-specific injection fields (`orig_data_*`/`inj_data_*` and `true_data_fnv`/`alt_data_fnv`). The audit therefore does not adopt the tool's class blindly: each model report quotes the model-specific record and states the rule. Conversely, the tool does not upgrade a pilot to `COMPLETE` merely because a consumer exists; DA04's v3.2.4 R04/R05 (SDC terminal, no chain in that trace) are `PARTIAL`.
+
+## 7. Answer to the brief's questions
+
+1. **Complete causal chains:** DA04 (2/5), LP01 (5/5), MD04 (3/5), FW05 (4/5); FW02 has one control-path chain (1/5).
+2. **Lifecycle/local evidence only:** SQ01, SQ03, SP05, LQ04 (all 5/5 pilots each).
+3. **Propagation-chain gaps:** A01, A03 (PARTIAL; no reference value / no address-matched crash), FW02 (4/5 partial), DA04/MD04/FW05 partial pilots for the reasons listed.
+4. **Terminal causality not yet proven:** A01 and A03 crashes (address mismatch); FW02 R01/R05 and MD04 R01/R04 squashes; DA04 v3.2.4 R04/R05 SDC terminal without a chain in that trace.
+5. **Supplementary experiments needed:** A01, A03, FW02, DA04 (raw oracle), MD04 (squash reason), FW05 (R01 squash), SQ01, SQ03, SP05 — see `SUPPLEMENTARY_PILOT_PLAN_v3.2.11.md`. None executed.
+6. **Models truly verified:** **4** (DA04, LP01, MD04, FW05). FW02 is control-path-only and is not counted as a data-effect verified model.
+7. **Verified pilots:** **14/55** unique pilots with a data-effect complete chain; **15/55** including FW02-R04's control-path chain.
+8. **Terminal split (14):** SDC **6**, MASKED **8**, SQUASHED **0**, CRASH **0**. Including FW02-R04: SDC 6, MASKED 9.
+
+**Formal 60-sample experiment status: NOT_STARTED.** No formal pilot was launched in this round.

+## 4. Terminal and causality
+
+R02/R03/R05 are complete control-effect chains: injection detail records violation_report_suppressed=1, the load nevertheless executes, receives data, writes back, and commits; its consumers receive the correct data (MASKED, oracle PASS). The fault effect is the suppressed violation report, not a corrupted value. R01/R04 are SQUASHED without proof that the squash is fault-caused (PARTIAL).
+
+## 5. Missing evidence
+
+ORACLE_FILE_MISSING in v3.2.6 (meta-sourced PASS); squash causality unproven in R01/R04
+
+## 6. Verdict
+
+- COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN: 3/5 pilots
+- PARTIAL: 2/5 pilots
+
+Model verdict: **COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN** (see per-pilot split above).
+
+## 7. Supplementary pilot recommendation
+
+Optional: trace the squash reason for R01/R04 to decide whether the squash is fault-caused; 2 runs after approval.

+# SQ01 Propagation Audit — v3.2.11
+
+**Model:** SQ01  **SubUnit:** SQ  **Audit date:** 2026-11-10  
+**Tool:** `tools/lsu_propagation_audit.py` + raw `trace_v31.jsonl` re-inspection  
+**Expected path:** Store data byte0 corruption → store_writeback → store_mem_write → a later Load reads the lane → memory_response → register_writeback → consumer → commit
+

+## 3.1 Model-specific evidence quote
+
+`site=lsq_write_sq_entry;mode=sq01_bit3;queue=SQ;index=1;generation=1;old_byte=0x0;new_byte=0x8;value_changed=1`.
+
+## 4. Terminal and causality
+
+All five pilots show the corrupted store data reaching store_writeback and the L1D write. No later load reads the corrupted lane in any pilot; the terminal is MASKED with reason STORE_COMMITTED_LANE_NOT_READ. The fault effect stops at the store-queue/cache state; it is not a data-propagation chain.
+
+## 5. Missing evidence
+
+NO_CONSUMER_EDGE (no later load of the lane) in 5/5; NO_EARLY_ANOMALY_EVENT (the trace has no explicit byte0 old/new anomaly node)
+
+## 6. Verdict
+
+- LIFECYCLE_ONLY: 5/5 pilots
+
+Model verdict: **LIFECYCLE_ONLY** (see per-pilot split above).
+
+## 7. Supplementary pilot recommendation
+
+A workload with a same-address reload after the store is required to test propagation: 5 pilots with preserved identities. Not executed this round.

+# Supplementary Pilot Plan (v3.2.11) — AWAITING_RESEARCHER_APPROVAL
+
+**Status:** No supplementary run was executed in this round. Every item below is a proposal only.
+**Rules applied (B8):** use the existing traces first; name the missing event; prove the current trace cannot supply it; name the hook/workload change; give the minimum run count; preserve the original SampleRunID and Seed; never overwrite a historical pilot; never mix binaries.
+
+## 1. Items
+
+### A01 — AGU wrong-EA reference value
+1. **Missing:** the data value at the *correct* EA (the trace records only the corrupted-EA response).
+2. **Why the current trace cannot supply it:** the injection record stores old/new EA but no reference read; the delivered value cannot be compared.
+3. **Change:** add a read-only reference-value hash at the original EA to the A01 trace hook (no change to the fault), and record the page-table-fault address with the injected VA.
+4. **Minimum runs:** 2 (one re-run of a masked pilot, one of the crash pilot).
+5. **Identity:** preserve the original SampleRunID/Seed; new output directory, no overwrite.
+6. **Decision it enables:** PARTIAL → COMPLETE for the pilots whose reference data differs; address-matched CRASH attribution.
+
+### A03 — AGU F5 stream + crash attribution
+1. **Missing:** writeback/consumer events in 5/6 pilots; an address-matched crash.
+2. **Why:** the v3.2.1 traces stop before writeback and the crash address is not the injected EA.
+3. **Change:** record the F5 eligible stream and the page-table-fault address in the trace (read-only additions).
+4. **Minimum runs:** 2 (one completed pilot, one crash pilot).
+5. **Identity:** preserved; no overwrite.
+
+### FW02 — complete the v3.2.4 pair
+1. **Missing:** consumer/commit records in the v3.2.4 R01/R05 traces; proof that the R01/R05 squashes are fault-caused.
+2. **Why:** the existing v3.2.4 traces lack the consumer node; the squash reason is not recorded.
+3. **Change:** trace the consumer and squash reason; no fault-definition change.
+4. **Minimum runs:** 2 (R01/R05 re-runs under the same seeds/ordinals).
+5. **Decision it enables:** whether FW02 has a data/control chain beyond R04.
+
+### DA04 — raw oracle files for the two complete chains
+1. **Missing:** raw `oracle.log`/`stdout.log` for `pilots_v3_2_6/DA04-R04,R05` (the SDC is meta/E2E-recorded).
+2. **Why:** the files are absent from the directories; the E2E evidence is a summary.
+3. **Change:** none to the fault; re-run the two preserved pilots and keep the raw oracle/stdout.
+4. **Minimum runs:** 2.
+5. **Decision it enables:** `OracleVerified=yes` at the raw-file level for the two SDC chains.
+
+### MD04 — squash causality
+1. **Missing:** the reason for the R01/R04 squash.
+2. **Change:** record the squash trigger/reason in the trace.
+3. **Minimum runs:** 2.
+4. **Decision it enables:** PARTIAL → COMPLETE if the squash is fault-caused, otherwise SQUASHED (still not a failure).
+
+### FW05 — R01 squash reason
+1. **Missing:** proof that the R01 squash is fault-caused.
+2. **Change:** trace the squash reason.
+3. **Minimum runs:** 1.
+
+### SQ01 / SQ03 — a consumer of the corrupted lane
+1. **Missing:** a later load of the same address after the store.
+2. **Why:** the current workload never reads the lane; the trace cannot manufacture a consumer.
+3. **Change:** a dedicated workload with a same-address reload after the store (new workload, new SHA, new golden oracle; old workload preserved).
+4. **Minimum runs:** 5 each (one per preserved SampleRunID/Seed).
+5. **Decision it enables:** data-propagation capability for the store-side models.
+
+### SP05 — entry-free/drain trace
+1. **Missing:** the entry-free/drain event and a consumer.
+2. **Change:** trace the entry release path (read-only hook addition); a consumer still needs a reload workload.
+3. **Minimum runs:** 1-2 (lifecycle) or 5 (with reload workload).
+
+### LQ04
+None. The literal definition is frozen and must not be changed to obtain SDC.
+
+## 2. Cost and risk
+
+- Binary changes: A01/A03/FW02/MD04/FW05/SP05 need trace-only additions; SQ01/SQ03/SP05 data propagation needs a new workload.
+- No C++ fault-definition change is proposed for any existing model.
+- Every run must use the preserved SampleRunID/Seed and a new output directory; historical artifacts are read-only.
+- None of these runs is authorized by this round. `Formal experiment = NOT_STARTED`.

+# A3 watchdog exit fix + runtime smoke (LSU v3.2.11, Task 5)
+
+Date: 2026-11-10. Worktree: `.claude/worktrees/lsu-platform-v3.2.11`, branch
+`lsu-platform-v3.2.11`. Baseline HEAD before this task: `83eaaf4c`.
+
+## 1. Root cause
+
+`CHAOSLQ04::watchdog()` (CHAOS/gem5/src/cpu/o3/CHAOSLQ04/CHAOSLQ04.cc:221)
+exited with `exitSimLoop("lq04 watchdog expired", 1)`. The stdlib
+`Simulator.run()` dispatches every exit event through
+`ExitEvent.translate_exit_status()` (gem5/src/python/gem5/simulate/exit_event.py,
+invoked from gem5/src/python/gem5/simulate/exit_handler.py:647), which raises
+
+```
+NotImplementedError: Exit event 'lq04 watchdog expired' not implemented
+```
+
+for any string outside its fixed table (exit_event.py:131). The registered
+string for a simulation failure is `"m5_fail instruction encountered"`
+(exit_event.py:91-92 -> `ExitEvent.FAIL`). The default FAIL handler is
+`exit_generator()` (exit_handler.py:556-563), so `Simulator.run()` returns
+normally instead of crashing the Python stack.
+
+A second defect compounded the first: `configs/se/lsu_proxy.py` ended with a
+bare `simulator.run()`, so even a FAIL exit carrying code 1 left the gem5
+process exiting 0 — the watchdog could never produce a clear non-zero exit
+status.
+
+## 2. The supported exit mechanism (the fix)
+
+1. `CHAOS/gem5/src/cpu/o3/CHAOSLQ04/CHAOSLQ04.cc` — the watchdog now calls
+   `exitSimLoop("m5_fail instruction encountered", 1);` (registered FAIL
+   event; the experimental reason stays in the CHAOS_LQ04_WATCHDOG_EXPIRED
+   log/stdout line directly above, which is unchanged).
+2. `configs/se/lsu_proxy.py` — after `simulator.run()`, the exit event code
+   is read via `simulator.get_last_exit_event_code()` and propagated with
+   `sys.exit(code)` ONLY when the exit cause is the registered FAIL event
+   (`"m5_fail instruction encountered"`). Rationale (review-fix round,
+   2026-11-10): the SE guest exit syscall also carries a status through
+   `exitSimLoop("exiting with last active thread context", guest_status)`
+   (syscall_emul.cc:250 -> ExitEvent.EXIT) — that is the workload's own
+   answer, not a simulator failure, so a guest whose `main()` returns
+   non-zero must still leave gem5 exiting 0; propagating its status would
+   make run_pilot misclassify a normal completion as
+   SIMULATOR_ERROR/CRASH/INCOMPLETE. Code-0 behavior is identical for every
+   cause. See §5.1 for the empirical control.
+
+## 3. Build
+
+The build trees (`build/`, `CHAOS/gem5/build/`) were copied with `cp -a` from
+the v3.2.10 worktree (identical C++ source under `CHAOS/gem5/src` per
+`git diff lsu-platform-v3.2.10..HEAD -- CHAOS/gem5/src`, empty). Three build
+findings, recorded for future rounds that copy build trees:
+
+- The copied scons configure cache (`build/ARM/gem5.build/scons_config` +
+  `sconsign.dblite`) does not survive the worktree move: the link-requiring
+  conftest checks (`-Wl,--as-needed`, linker `-gz`, `CheckPythonLib`) miss the
+  SConf decision cache and try to relink conftest executables whose
+  intermediate `.o` files were cleaned as scons intermediates ->
+  `ld: cannot find ... .o` -> `Error: Can't find a working Python installation`.
+  Fix: `scons -j16 --config=force build/ARM/gem5.opt` (SConf clones the env
+  for configure only; the main build decider is untouched). All fresh check
+  outcomes matched the v3.2.10 build exactly (as-needed yes, Python 3.11.6,
+  tcmalloc/protobuf yes, no capstone/png/hdf5).
+- The forced configure invalidated the generated headers, so ~2583 targets
+  recompiled (including `src/cpu/o3/CHAOSLQ04/CHAOSLQ04.cc`, cleanly, no new
+  warnings; the only warning lines are the pre-existing
+  "Unrecognized architecture for systemc" and missing-optional-lib notes that
+  also appear in the v3.2.10 log).
+- SCons then skipped the final link: the copied `sconsign.dblite` still
+  vouched for the stale `gem5.opt` (its stored dep signature
+  `db6adcd8...` for CHAOSLQ04.o vs the new `e8c2fbcf...` on disk). Removing
+  the stale binary and re-running scons produced the clean final link.
+
+Build output tail (`logs_v3_2_11/build_watchdog.log`, final run):
+
+```
+ [     CXX] src/base/date.cc -> ARM/base/date.o
+scons: done building targets.
+```
+(with `[    LINK]  -> ARM/gem5.opt` at log line 1116; exit status 0)
+
+Binary SHA256 (both copies identical, `logs_v3_2_11/binary_v3211_watchdog.sha256`):
+
+```
+3dccaa10ec5073ca90f2622bd37d5adac1b705bd9faf2497c2d13e46beadd4ed  CHAOS/gem5/build/ARM/gem5.opt
+3dccaa10ec5073ca90f2622bd37d5adac1b705bd9faf2497c2d13e46beadd4ed  build/ARM/gem5.opt
+```
+
+(The stale copy this replaced was byte-identical to v3.2.10's
+`f3c2a4423aab81ca565c7adcfb840a7b8f9cdb9f2a6b6b5ecc3d0e148af20694`; the old
+file was moved to `/tmp/gem5_opt_stale_v3210_copy` during the relink.)
+

+## 5. Watchdog smoke
+
+Disk checked first (`df -h /home`: 78G free > 70.6 GiB). Command:
+
+```
+build/ARM/gem5.opt --outdir=/tmp/lq04_watchdog_smoke configs/se/lsu_proxy.py \
+  --cpu O3 --variant B0 --chaos_propagation --prop_trace_file prop_trace.csv.gz \
+  --chaos_lq04 --lq04_event_ordinal 999 --lq04_max_faults 1 \
+  --lq04_watchdog_cycles 20000 \
+  --cmd=$PWD/docs/gem5-fi/lsu/implementation_v3_2/workloads/lq_lifetime_ordinal
+```
+
+- Exit code: `1`. Before the fix this run instead died mid-run with an
+  unhandled `NotImplementedError` traceback; with only the C++ fix it would
+  have exited the FAIL event cleanly but with process status 0 — the config
+  change is what propagates the event's code 1 to the process exit status.
+- stdout tail (`watchdog_smoke/stdout.log`):
+
+```
+[lsu_proxy] B0 caches applied (variant=B0): L1D 32KiB/2-way lat222 MSHR6/t8/wb16 + L1I 32KiB/2 lat111 MSHR2 + L2 1MiB/16 lat12 MSHR16 + StridePrefetcher(8,1,on)@L2
+CHAOS_LQ04_WATCHDOG_EXPIRED: selected=0 injected=0 eligible=821 ordinal=999 tick=7700000
+```
+
+- stderr (`watchdog_smoke/stderr.log`): 8 lines, all standard gem5 warnings
+  (dot file, DRAM capacity, se_workload OS, legacy stats, bucket sizes, gdb
+  sockets). **No `NotImplementedError`.**
+- Trace census (`watchdog_smoke/trace_census.txt`): `prop_trace.csv.gz`
+  exists (284931 bytes), `gzip -t` OK — the exit callbacks closed the gzip
+  stream cleanly; 10012 lines = 1 header + 10011 events; first event tick
+  78540, last valid event retained at tick 7699615 (immediately before the
+  watchdog at tick 7700000):
+
+```
+10010,7699615,0,lq_lifecycle,31674,,,816,,queue=LQ;action=free;index=816;generation=826;reason=commit
+```
+
+### 5.1 Review fix: scope the exit-code propagation to the watchdog FAIL event
+
+The first version of the `lsu_proxy.py` tail propagated ANY non-zero
+exit-event code. Review finding (2026-11-10): the SE guest exit syscall
+carries the guest's own status through
+`exitSimLoop("exiting with last active thread context", guest_status)`
+(syscall_emul.cc:250), so a future workload whose `main()` returns non-zero
+would have exited gem5 non-zero and run_pilot would have misclassified a
+normal completion as SIMULATOR_ERROR/CRASH/INCOMPLETE. The earlier
+"normal completions carry code 0" rationale was wrong in general — it only
+held because every current workload happens to return 0.
+
+Fix (config only, binary unchanged, sha
+`3dccaa10ec5073ca90f2622bd37d5adac1b705bd9faf2497c2d13e46beadd4ed`):
+propagate only when BOTH the code is non-zero AND the cause is
+`"m5_fail instruction encountered"` (the CHAOS watchdog's registered FAIL
+event). Code-0 behavior is identical for every cause.
+
+Re-verification (evidence under `watchdog_smoke/after_fix_*`, first smoke
+evidence kept untouched):
+
+- Watchdog smoke re-run (same command, outdir
+  `/tmp/lq04_watchdog_smoke_after_fix`): `exit=1`,
+  `CHAOS_LQ04_WATCHDOG_EXPIRED` present, no `NotImplementedError`
+  (0 occurrences in stdout+stderr, 8 standard warning lines), trace
+  byte-identical in size/content to the first smoke (284931 bytes, 1 header
+  + 10011 events, last valid event retained at tick 7699615;
+  `after_fix_trace_census.txt`), classification replay
+  `CLASSIFICATION_CHECK: PASS` — HANG/INCOMPLETE, never MASKED
+  (`after_fix_classify_check.txt`).
+- Normal completion (same command minus `--chaos_lq04`/watchdog flags, the
+  LQ04 workload running fault-free to its guest exit): `exit=0`, final
+  checksum `0xb097eb83f71545cb` printed (`after_fix_normal_*`).
+- Guest-status control (`after_fix_guest_status_control.txt`): a scratch
+  static AArch64 guest `int main(void){return 3;}` (host gcc -O2 -static,
+  built in /tmp only) exits gem5 with **0** under the fixed config but with
+  **3** under the pre-fix config (`git show HEAD:configs/se/lsu_proxy.py`,
+  sha256 `615ff1ff...`) — the trap the reviewer flagged is reproduced
+  pre-fix and closed post-fix, while the watchdog FAIL path still exits 1.
+
+## 6. Classification check (`watchdog_smoke/classify_check.{py,txt}`)
+
+The harness replays the exact `run_pilot.py` watchdog branch
+(`CHAOS_LQ04_WATCHDOG_EXPIRED` in stdout/stderr, before the generic
+non-zero-returncode CRASH branch) on the real smoke outputs, converts the
+real trace through the platform pipeline (`tools.prop_trace_to_v31.convert`),
+and calls `terminal_rules.adjudicate` exactly as run_pilot.py:885-889 does:
+
+```
+gem5 exit code            : 1
+NotImplementedError seen  : False
+watchdog_hit              : True
+injected (lq04 log)       : 0
+run_status/activation     : SIMULATOR_ERROR/NOT_TRIGGERED
+terminal/completeness     : HANG/INCOMPLETE
+rows=10011 truncated=False
+final_terminal            : HANG          (terminal_rules.adjudicate, observed_terminal=HANG)
+terminal_reason           : None
+MASKED in verdict         : False
+adversarial probe (oracle_pass=True): final_terminal=HANG, MASKED in verdict: False
+CLASSIFICATION_CHECK: PASS
+```
+
+`HANG` is in `terminal_rules.HARD_TERMINALS`, so `adjudicate` returns it
+before any MASKED/SDC path can be reached — a watchdog run can never be
+adjudicated MASKED, even if an oracle had passed. The converted trace closes
+with `terminal=HANG`, `completeness=INCOMPLETE`, `truncated=False` (clean
+gzip EOF, last valid event retained).
+
+## 7. Verdict
+
+A3 fixed and runtime-verified: the watchdog now exits through gem5's
+registered FAIL event, the gem5 process fails with exit code 1 and the
+CHAOS_LQ04_WATCHDOG_EXPIRED reason line, the propagation trace is retained
+intact, and the platform adjudication of such a run is HANG/INCOMPLETE —
+never MASKED.

+== after_fix guest-status control: a non-zero-returning guest must NOT fail gem5 ==
+Date: 2026-11-10 (Task 5 review-fix round). Binary: build/ARM/gem5.opt
+sha256 3dccaa10ec5073ca90f2622bd37d5adac1b705bd9faf2497c2d13e46beadd4ed
+(unchanged from the Task-5 build; only configs/se/lsu_proxy.py changed, no rebuild).
+
+Reviewed finding under test: the pre-fix lsu_proxy.py propagated ANY non-zero
+exit-event code via sys.exit. But the SE guest exit syscall carries the guest's
+own status through exitSimLoop("exiting with last active thread context",
+status & 0xff) (CHAOS/gem5/src/sim/syscall_emul.cc:250, mapped to
+ExitEvent.EXIT at gem5/src/python/gem5/simulate/exit_event.py:83). A workload
+whose main() returns non-zero would therefore have exited gem5 non-zero, and
+run_pilot.py's generic non-zero-returncode branch would misclassify a normal
+completion as SIMULATOR_ERROR/CRASH/INCOMPLETE.
+
+Scratch guest workload (built in /tmp, host gcc -O2 -static per repo recipe;
+NOT added to the repo, no historical pilot touched):
+
+  $ printf 'int main(void) { return 3; }\n' > /tmp/guest_exit3.c
+  $ gcc -O2 -static -o /tmp/guest_exit3 /tmp/guest_exit3.c
+  $ /tmp/guest_exit3; echo "native_exit=$?"
+  native_exit=3
+
+1) PRE-FIX control (config = git HEAD c6e2c55c-era file, verified identical
+   to `git show HEAD:configs/se/lsu_proxy.py`, sha256
+   615ff1ff908f13898e817e2b38123be9f4d9b0db54c4382796675be5e39b2438):
+
+  $ build/ARM/gem5.opt --outdir=/tmp/lq04_guest_exit3_prefix \
+      /tmp/lsu_proxy_prefix.py --cpu O3 --variant B0 --cmd=/tmp/guest_exit3
+  prefix_gem5_exit=3        <-- the trap is REAL: guest status 3 leaked into
+                                the gem5 process exit status
+
+2) FIXED config (this round: propagation scoped to cause
+   "m5_fail instruction encountered" AND non-zero code):
+
+  $ build/ARM/gem5.opt --outdir=/tmp/lq04_guest_exit3_after_fix \
+      configs/se/lsu_proxy.py --cpu O3 --variant B0 --cmd=/tmp/guest_exit3
+  gem5_exit=0               <-- guest status no longer propagated; normal
+                                completion semantics restored
+
+3) Watchdog FAIL path still propagates (same binary, fixed config; full
+   evidence in after_fix_{stdout,stderr,exit_code,trace_census,
+   classify_check}.*):
+
+  $ build/ARM/gem5.opt --outdir=/tmp/lq04_watchdog_smoke_after_fix \
+      configs/se/lsu_proxy.py --cpu O3 --variant B0 --chaos_propagation \
+      --prop_trace_file prop_trace.csv.gz --chaos_lq04 \
+      --lq04_event_ordinal 999 --lq04_max_faults 1 \
+      --lq04_watchdog_cycles 20000 --cmd=<lq_lifetime_ordinal>
+  exit=1                    <-- ExitEvent.FAIL ("m5_fail instruction
+                                encountered") IS propagated
+
+GUEST_STATUS_CONTROL: PASS (pre-fix exit=3 reproduced the trap; fixed exit=0;
+watchdog FAIL exit=1 retained)

+"""Task 5 Step 5: classification check for the LQ04 watchdog smoke (v3.2.11).
+
+Replays, on the REAL smoke outputs, the exact branch semantics of
+``run_pilot.py`` (watchdog_hit -> HANG/INCOMPLETE) and the platform
+adjudication (``terminal_rules.adjudicate``), then proves the verdict is
+HANG and can never be MASKED -- including the adversarial case where the
+oracle would have passed.
+
+Usage: python3 classify_check.py <smoke_outdir> <stdout_log> <stderr_log> <exit_code>
+"""
+import json
+import sys
+from pathlib import Path
+
+PLATFORM = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2] / "platform"
+sys.path.insert(0, str(PLATFORM))
+
+import run_pilot  # noqa: E402  (for _lq04_events only)
+import terminal_rules  # noqa: E402
+from tools.prop_trace_to_v31 import convert  # noqa: E402
+
+out_dir = Path(sys.argv[1])
+stdout_log = Path(sys.argv[2])
+stderr_log = Path(sys.argv[3])
+exit_code = int(sys.argv[4])
+
+proc_stdout = stdout_log.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
+proc_stderr = stderr_log.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
+
+# --- run_pilot.py branch replay (lines 804-858), verbatim semantics -----
+events = run_pilot._lq04_events(out_dir / "lq04_injections.log")
+injections = [e for e in events if e.get("kind") == "injection"]
+injected = len(injections)
+
+crash_by_fault = False  # LQ04 is not in ("A01", "A03", "FW02")
+timed_out = False       # no external wall-clock timeout fired; exit was clean
+
+watchdog_hit = ("CHAOS_SQ03_WATCHDOG_EXPIRED" in (proc_stdout + proc_stderr)
+                or "CHAOS_SP05_WATCHDOG_EXPIRED" in (proc_stdout + proc_stderr)
+                or "CHAOS_LQ04_WATCHDOG_EXPIRED" in (proc_stdout + proc_stderr))
+notimplemented = "NotImplementedError" in (proc_stdout + proc_stderr)
+
+if timed_out:
+    run_status, activation, terminal, completeness = (
+        "TIMEOUT", "TRIGGERED" if injected else "NOT_TRIGGERED",
+        "HANG", "INCOMPLETE")
+elif watchdog_hit:
+    run_status, activation, terminal, completeness = (
+        "SIMULATOR_ERROR", "TRIGGERED" if injected else "NOT_TRIGGERED",
+        "HANG", "INCOMPLETE")
+elif exit_code != 0 and crash_by_fault:
+    run_status, activation, terminal, completeness = (
+        "SIMULATOR_ERROR", "TRIGGERED", "CRASH", "COMPLETE")
+elif exit_code != 0:
+    run_status, activation, terminal, completeness = (
+        "SIMULATOR_ERROR", "TRIGGERED" if injected else "NOT_TRIGGERED",
+        "CRASH", "INCOMPLETE")
+else:
+    # run_pilot's tail branch (unreachable here: watchdog_hit is True)
+    oracle_pass_tail = False
+    run_status, activation, terminal, completeness = (
+        "COMPLETED", "TRIGGERED" if injected else "NOT_TRIGGERED",
+        "SDC" if not oracle_pass_tail else "MASKED", "COMPLETE")
+
+print("== run_pilot branch replay on the real smoke outputs ==")
+print(f"gem5 exit code            : {exit_code}")
+print(f"NotImplementedError seen  : {notimplemented}")
+print(f"watchdog_hit              : {watchdog_hit}")
+print(f"injected (lq04 log)       : {injected}")
+print(f"run_status/activation     : {run_status}/{activation}")
+print(f"terminal/completeness     : {terminal}/{completeness}")
+
+# --- convert the real trace exactly like run_pilot lines 860-873 --------
+csv_path = out_dir / "prop_trace.csv.gz"
+jsonl_path = out_dir / "trace_v31.jsonl"
+converted = {"rows": 0, "truncated": True, "summary": None}
+if csv_path.exists():
+    try:
+        converted = convert(
+            csv_path, jsonl_path,
+            sample_run_id="watchdog-smoke-v3211", run_id="watchdog-smoke-v3211",
+            sub_task_id="LQ04", fault_id="LQ04", fault_model_id="LQ04",
+            run_status=run_status, activation=activation, terminal=terminal,
+            completeness=completeness, allow_truncated_complete=crash_by_fault)
+    except SystemExit as exc:
+        print(f"convert refused: {exc}")
+
+print("\n== trace conversion (real pipeline path) ==")
+print(f"rows={converted['rows']} truncated={converted['truncated']}")
+
+# --- platform adjudication (run_pilot lines 884-889) --------------------
+trace_events = run_pilot._read_events(jsonl_path)
+oracle_pass = False  # the workload never completed: no oracle run is possible
+final_terminal, terminal_reason = terminal_rules.adjudicate(
+    trace_events, model="LQ04", observed_terminal=terminal,
+    oracle_pass=oracle_pass,
+    trace_complete=(completeness == "COMPLETE"))
+
+print("\n== terminal_rules.adjudicate(observed_terminal=HANG) ==")
+print(f"events read back          : {len(trace_events)}")
+print(f"final_terminal            : {final_terminal}")
+print(f"terminal_reason           : {terminal_reason}")
+print(f"MASKED in verdict         : {'MASKED' == final_terminal}")
+
+# --- adversarial probe: even a passing oracle cannot make it MASKED -----
+adv_terminal, adv_reason = terminal_rules.adjudicate(
+    trace_events, model="LQ04", observed_terminal=terminal,
+    oracle_pass=True, trace_complete=False)
+print("\n== adversarial probe (oracle_pass=True) ==")
+print(f"final_terminal            : {adv_terminal}")
+print(f"terminal_reason           : {adv_reason}")
+print(f"MASKED in verdict         : {'MASKED' == adv_terminal}")
+
+ok = (exit_code == 1 and watchdog_hit and not notimplemented
+      and final_terminal == "HANG" and adv_terminal == "HANG"
+      and "MASKED" not in (final_terminal, adv_terminal))
+print("\nCLASSIFICATION_CHECK: " + ("PASS" if ok else "FAIL"))
+sys.exit(0 if ok else 1)
