+"""Fault-effect propagation coverage (LSU v3.2.11 Task 2 / A1).
+
+``trace_completeness.propagation_coverage`` (aliased as
+``structural_chain_completeness`` since v3.2.11) answers a STRUCTURAL
+question: did the expected stage kinds appear on the fault's own chain?
+This module answers the different, EFFECT question: did the injected fault
+actually diverge a value or a control decision, and did that divergence
+reach a committed architectural outcome?
+
+The two questions are deliberately separate.  A structurally complete chain
+can carry an intact payload end to end (LQ04 is the canonical case: the
+fault corrupts LQ-entry lifetime metadata, the response is bound by
+senderState, and the delivered data is unchanged by construction), so the
+honest effect grade for such a run is LIFECYCLE_ONLY, not COMPLETE.
+
+Classification rules (v3.2.11 A1 spec):
+
+- The chain is ONLY the events whose ``ptag`` equals the injection's
+  ``ptag`` or whose ``dyn_inst_seq`` equals the injection's
+  ``dyn_inst_seq`` (mirrors ``terminal_rules.chain_events``, with ``str()``
+  normalization because numeric identity fields are ints in some trace
+  vintages and JSON strings in others).  Time adjacency NEVER links events.
+- ``value_diverged``: a chained ``register_writeback`` with
+  ``data_corruption=1`` or ``value_matches_payload=0``; a chained
+  ``consumer_commit`` with ``consumer_input_matches_source=0``; a chained
+  ``consumer_commit`` whose own ``source_value_hash`` differs from its
+  ``consumer_input_fnv`` when both exist; or a chained ``consumer_commit``
+  whose ``consumer_input_fnv`` differs from its source event's
+  ``writeback_value_fnv``/``data_fnv``/``consumer_output_fnv`` when both
+  exist (``consumer_output_fnv`` carries the source hash on
+  consumer->consumer re-taint edges, which dominate real traces).  "Both
+  exist" means both are REAL hashes: the converter writes the literal
+  string ``null`` into hash fields it has no value for (older vintages
+  wrote ``0``, e.g. ``forward_data_fnv=0``), so every hash field is first
+  normalized through ``_real_hash`` and a sentinel/empty/missing field is
+  ABSENT -- a divergence is never fabricated from an absent hash.
+- ``control_diverged``: a chained event whose ``detail`` contains any
+  marker key from ``EFFECT_MARKERS[model]``.  Marker scanning is
+  presence-based per the A1 spec; the marker VALUE is recorded in the
+  evidence so the v3.2.11 audit round can refine it (for FW02 the
+  refinement is ``fault_decision != none`` -- equivalently
+  ``decision != normal_decision``: real traces record
+  ``fault_decision=none`` on unaffected forwarding nodes and
+  ``fault_decision=suppressed`` under the fault, whereas comparing
+  ``fault_decision`` against ``normal_decision`` would flag every node).
+- ``COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN`` only when a divergence exists AND a chained
+  commit/errant_commit/consumer_commit or an oracle-failing terminal (SDC)
+  explains it; ``PARTIAL`` when divergence/control evidence exists without
+  that downstream link; ``LIFECYCLE_ONLY`` when the injection is TRIGGERED
+  and nothing diverged; ``UNRESOLVED`` when there is no injection, the
+  terminal is missing/UNRESOLVED, the trace declares itself INCOMPLETE, no
+  summary record exists, or the injection never activated.
+"""
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+# Control-flow effect marker keys per fault model: a chained event whose
+# detail carries the key is control-divergence evidence for that model.
+# v3.2.11 Task 2 seeds the models covered by the tests; the audit round
+# (Task 6/7) extends and value-refines the table from real traces.
+EFFECT_MARKERS: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {
+    # LQ04 corrupts LQ-entry lifetime metadata (valid+request bits).  Its
+    # observable effect IS the entry-lifetime window; the delivered data is
+    # unchanged by construction, so no control marker exists and intact
+    # LQ04 chains grade LIFECYCLE_ONLY.
+    "LQ04": (),
+    # MD04 suppresses a memory-order violation report.  The suppression is
+    # recorded on the injection node (verified: pilots_v3_2_6/MD04-R01).
+    "MD04": ("violation_report_suppressed",),
+    # FW02 corrupts the store->load forwarding decision; the forwarding
+    # node records the decision taken under the fault (verified:
+    # pilots_v3_2_4/FW02-R01).
+    "FW02": ("fault_decision",),
+    # SQ01 flips a bit of a store's data byte (verified:
+    # pilots_v3_2_3/SQ01-R01 log, converter kind inj_store_data): a pure
+    # value-domain fault whose divergence is captured by the hash checks,
+    # with no control decision to mark.
+    "SQ01": (),
+}
+
+# Terminals that by themselves explain a divergence: the oracle observed
+# wrong output.  MASKED/SQUASHED/CRASH do not -- a crash may be unrelated
+# and a squash erases the effect before the oracle sees it.
+ORACLE_FAILING_TERMINALS = ("SDC",)
+
+# Event types that turn a divergence into a committed architectural
+# outcome.  consumer_commit is included: it records a consumer
+# instruction's commit with its input/output values.
+DOWNSTREAM_COMMIT_TYPES = ("commit", "errant_commit", "consumer_commit")
+
+# Cap on the per-item divergence evidence lists; the counts stay exact.
+_EVIDENCE_CAP = 20
+
+_VALUE_FLAGS = (
+    # (event_type, detail key, divergent value, evidence kind)
+    ("register_writeback", "data_corruption", "1", "data_corruption"),
+    ("register_writeback", "value_matches_payload", "0",
+     "value_matches_payload"),
+    ("consumer_commit", "consumer_input_matches_source", "0",
+     "consumer_input_matches_source"),
+)
+# Source-event detail keys that can carry the hash of the value a consumer
+# read.  Writeback-sourced edges carry it in writeback_value_fnv/data_fnv;
+# consumer->consumer re-taint edges (the vast majority on real traces, e.g.
+# 325 of the 326 consumer edges on pilots_v3_2_10/LQ04-R01) carry it in the
+# source consumer's consumer_output_fnv.
+_SOURCE_HASH_KEYS = ("writeback_value_fnv", "data_fnv", "consumer_output_fnv")
+
+# Values the trace converter writes into a hash field when no hash exists
+# for that edge: the current converter writes the literal string "null"
+# (LP01 class=cc consumer nodes carry source_value_hash=null /
+# consumer_output_fnv=null next to a real consumer_input_fnv), older
+# vintages wrote "0" (seen as forward_data_fnv=0).  Truthiness-only guards
+# read these sentinels as real hashes and fabricated 204 null-artifact
+# evidence items archive-wide before _real_hash existed -- on
+# pilots_v3_2_5/LP01-R01 every one of the 96 hash-based items was a
+# "null"-vs-real comparison.  Comparison is case-insensitive so "None"
+# (Python-repr style) is covered too; "0" cannot collide with a real FNV
+# hash because these fields carry full fixed-width hex strings.
+_ABSENT_HASH_SENTINELS = frozenset(("null", "none", "0", ""))
+
+
+def _real_hash(value) -> str | None:
+    """Normalize a hash field to a real hash string, or None when absent.
+
+    Absent means: the key is missing (None), the field is empty or
+    whitespace-only, or it holds one of the converter's null sentinels
+    ("null"/"None", older "0").  Every hash comparison in this module goes
+    through this helper, so a sentinel is never read as a real hash and a
+    divergence is never fabricated from an absent hash.
+    """
+    if value is None:
+        return None
+    text = str(value).strip()
+    if text.lower() in _ABSENT_HASH_SENTINELS:
+        return None
+    return text
+
+
+def _detail(event: dict) -> dict:
+    return dict(item.split("=", 1) for item in (event.get("detail") or "")
+                .split(";") if "=" in item)
+
+
+def _is_event(record: dict) -> bool:
+    """Event records only: excludes edges, summaries and parse-error
+    placeholders (which carry no record_type and no event_type)."""
+    if "record_type" in record:
+        return record["record_type"] == "event"
+    return "event_type" in record
+
+
+def _chain_events(events: list[dict], injection: dict) -> list[dict]:
+    """Fault's own chain: same ptag or same dyn_inst_seq, str()-normalized.
+
+    ptag 0/""/None carries no chain identity (platform convention: commit,
+    squash and lifecycle nodes are written with ptag=0); such injections
+    fall back to the dyn_inst_seq identity alone.
+    """
+    ptag = injection.get("ptag")
+    seq = injection.get("dyn_inst_seq")
+    ptag_s = None if ptag in (None, "") or str(ptag) == "0" else str(ptag)
+    seq_s = None if seq in (None, "") else str(seq)
+    chain = []
+    for e in events:
+        if ptag_s is not None and str(e.get("ptag")) == ptag_s:
+            chain.append(e)
+        elif seq_s is not None and str(e.get("dyn_inst_seq")) == seq_s:
+            chain.append(e)
+    return chain
+
+
+def classify(model: str, events: list[dict], injection: dict | None,
+             terminal: str) -> dict:
+    """Grade the fault-effect propagation of one trace.
+
+    ``events`` is the record list (event records plus, when available, the
+    summary record); ``injection`` is the injection event record or None
+    (controls); ``terminal`` is the adjudicated terminal state.
+    """
+    records = list(events or [])
+    evt = [r for r in records if _is_event(r)]
+    summaries = [r for r in records if r.get("record_type") == "summary"]
+    summary = summaries[-1] if summaries else {}
+
+    def _unresolved(reason: str, evidence: dict) -> dict:
+        return {"fault_effect_propagation_coverage": "UNRESOLVED",
+                "fault_effect_evidence": evidence,
+                "fault_effect_reason": reason}
+
+    if injection is None:
+        # Controls (no injection event) and injection-less traces.
+        return _unresolved("NO_INJECTION_EVENT", {
+            "model": model,
+            "chain_identity": "ptag+dyn_inst_seq (str-normalized)",
+            "chain_events": 0,
+        })
+
+    # The chain exists as soon as the injection does; every later path
+    # (including the UNRESOLVED gates) reports truthful chain statistics and
+    # the chain-scoped divergence observation.
+    chain = _chain_events(evt, injection)
+    by_id = {}
+    for e in evt:
+        if e.get("event_id") is not None:
+            by_id.setdefault(str(e["event_id"]), e)
+
+    value_divergence: list[dict] = []
+    control_divergence: list[dict] = []
+
+    def _value(ev: dict, kind: str, **extra) -> None:
+        item = {"event_id": ev.get("event_id"),
+                "event_type": ev.get("event_type"), "kind": kind}
+        item.update(extra)
+        value_divergence.append(item)
+
+    for e in chain:
+        et = e.get("event_type")
+        d = _detail(e)
+        for ev_type, key, divergent, kind in _VALUE_FLAGS:
+            if et == ev_type and str(d.get(key)) == divergent:
+                _value(e, kind, **{key: d.get(key)})
+        if et == "consumer_commit":
+            # Both hash guards normalize through _real_hash first: the
+            # converter's "null"/"None"/"0"/empty sentinels are ABSENT
+            # hashes, never hashes to compare against.
+            consumer_fnv = _real_hash(d.get("consumer_input_fnv"))
+            # Own-edge check: the re-taint bookkeeping hash of the source
+            # value vs. what this consumer actually read.  Independent of
+            # the source event's records, so it also fires on edges whose
+            # source event is missing or carries no source hash.
+            own_hash = _real_hash(d.get("source_value_hash"))
+            if consumer_fnv and own_hash and own_hash != consumer_fnv:
+                _value(e, "source_value_hash_mismatch",
+                       consumer_input_fnv=consumer_fnv,
+                       source_value_hash=own_hash)
+            src = by_id.get(str(e.get("source_event_id")))
+            if consumer_fnv and src is not None:
+                sd = _detail(src)
+                for hk in _SOURCE_HASH_KEYS:
+                    source_hash = _real_hash(sd.get(hk))
+                    if source_hash and source_hash != consumer_fnv:
+                        _value(e, "consumer_input_fnv_mismatch",
+                               consumer_input_fnv=consumer_fnv,
+                               source_event_id=e.get("source_event_id"),
+                               source_hash_kind=hk,
+                               source_hash=source_hash)
+
+    markers = EFFECT_MARKERS.get(model, ())
+    for e in chain:
+        d = _detail(e)
+        for marker in markers:
+            if marker in d:
+                control_divergence.append(
+                    {"event_id": e.get("event_id"),
+                     "event_type": e.get("event_type"),
+                     "marker": marker, "marker_value": d.get(marker)})
+
+    def _gate(reason: str) -> dict:
+        return _unresolved(reason, {
+            "model": model,
+            "chain_identity": "ptag+dyn_inst_seq (str-normalized)",
+            "injection_event_id": injection.get("event_id"),
+            "activation_status": (injection.get("activation_status")
+                                  or summary.get("fault_activation_status")),
+            "chain_ptag": injection.get("ptag"),
+            "chain_dyn_inst_seq": injection.get("dyn_inst_seq"),
+            "chain_events": len(chain),
+            "chain_event_types": sorted({str(e.get("event_type"))
+                                         for e in chain}),
+            "value_divergence_count": len(value_divergence),
+            "value_divergence": value_divergence[:_EVIDENCE_CAP],
+            "control_divergence_count": len(control_divergence),
+            "control_divergence": control_divergence[:_EVIDENCE_CAP],
+        })
+
+    # -- UNRESOLVED gates, in order ------------------------------------
+    if terminal is None:
+        return _gate("TERMINAL_MISSING")
+    if str(terminal).upper() == "UNRESOLVED":
+        return _gate("TERMINAL_UNRESOLVED")
+    if not summaries:
+        return _gate("NO_SUMMARY_RECORD")
+    if summary.get("trace_completeness") == "INCOMPLETE":
+        return _gate("TRACE_DECLARED_INCOMPLETE")
+
+    activation = (injection.get("activation_status")
+                  or summary.get("fault_activation_status"))
+    if str(activation).upper() != "TRIGGERED":
+        # A dormant injection produced no fault effect to resolve.
+        return _gate("INJECTION_NOT_TRIGGERED")
+
+    # -- downstream explanation -----------------------------------------
+    commit_types = sorted({str(e.get("event_type")) for e in chain
+                           if e.get("event_type") in DOWNSTREAM_COMMIT_TYPES})
+    oracle_failing = str(terminal).upper() in ORACLE_FAILING_TERMINALS
+
+    evidence = {
+        "model": model,
+        "chain_identity": "ptag+dyn_inst_seq (str-normalized)",
+        "injection_event_id": injection.get("event_id"),
+        "activation_status": activation,
+        "chain_ptag": injection.get("ptag"),
+        "chain_dyn_inst_seq": injection.get("dyn_inst_seq"),
+        "chain_events": len(chain),
+        "chain_event_types": sorted({str(e.get("event_type"))
+                                     for e in chain}),
+        "control_markers": list(markers),
+        "value_divergence_count": len(value_divergence),
+        "value_divergence": value_divergence[:_EVIDENCE_CAP],
+        "control_divergence_count": len(control_divergence),
+        "control_divergence": control_divergence[:_EVIDENCE_CAP],
+        "downstream_commit_types": commit_types,
+        "oracle_failing_terminal": oracle_failing,
+    }
+    if len(value_divergence) > _EVIDENCE_CAP:
+        evidence["value_divergence_truncated"] = True
+    if len(control_divergence) > _EVIDENCE_CAP:
+        evidence["control_divergence_truncated"] = True
+
+    diverged = bool(value_divergence or control_divergence)
+    if diverged and (commit_types or oracle_failing):
+        reason = ("DIVERGENCE_WITH_ORACLE_FAILING_TERMINAL" if oracle_failing
+                  and not commit_types
+                  else "DIVERGENCE_WITH_DOWNSTREAM_COMMIT")
+        return {"fault_effect_propagation_coverage": "COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN",
+                "fault_effect_evidence": evidence,
+                "fault_effect_reason": reason}
+    if diverged:
+        return {"fault_effect_propagation_coverage": "PARTIAL",
+                "fault_effect_evidence": evidence,
+                "fault_effect_reason": "DIVERGENCE_WITHOUT_DOWNSTREAM_LINK"}
+    return {"fault_effect_propagation_coverage": "LIFECYCLE_ONLY",
+            "fault_effect_evidence": evidence,
+            "fault_effect_reason": "TRIGGERED_NO_DIVERGENCE"}

+"""Run-role semantics for the LSU pilot platform (LSU v3.2.11 Task 4 / A4).
+
+A pilot is either a FAULT run or a matched no-fault CONTROL, decided by the
+CLI ``--probability``.  The two roles have different -- and equally strict --
+validity semantics:
+
+* FAULT: the injector must have fired and the record must carry a resolved
+  terminal verdict (gated in ``run_pilot.main`` as ``fault_valid``).
+* CONTROL: the injector must NOT have fired.  A control is valid when the
+  process exited cleanly, the un-faulted workload passed its oracle, zero
+  injections were recorded, and the converted trace is COMPLETE with no
+  validator errors.
+
+Acceptance is role-exclusive (``compute_acceptance``): a CONTROL is judged
+only by the control gate and a FAULT run only by the fault gate.  Recorded
+controller ruling (Task 4 fix loop): this supersedes the brief's literal
+``fault_valid or control_valid`` disjunction, under which a CONTROL whose
+injector misfired (``injection_count > 0`` -- reachable for ordinal-gated
+injectors run with ``--probability 0.0``) could be accepted through the
+fault criteria even though it is an invalid control.  A control is not a
+fault run.
+
+Before v3.2.11 the fault-run expression was applied to controls too, so a
+perfectly healthy no-fault control was recorded as ``accepted: false`` (the
+A4 defect).  A control is a record of absence: its honest terminal stays
+``UNRESOLVED``/``NO_INJECTION_EVENT`` (never ``MASKED``) and its activation
+status is ``NOT_TRIGGERED``; only its validity gate changes.
+"""
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+
+def classify_run_role(probability: float) -> str:
+    """Return "CONTROL" for a zero-probability run, "FAULT" otherwise."""
+    return "CONTROL" if probability == 0.0 else "FAULT"
+
+
+def control_valid(*, exit_code: int, oracle_pass: bool,
+                  injection_count: int, trace_completeness: str,
+                  validator_errors: list[str]) -> bool:
+    """A matched no-fault control is valid iff nothing went wrong.
+
+    True iff the process exited 0, the workload oracle passed, no injection
+    was recorded, the converted trace is COMPLETE and the trace validator
+    reported no errors.  Anything else (a crash, an oracle failure on the
+    un-faulted run, an injector that fired anyway, a broken trace) makes
+    the control record invalid.
+    """
+    return (exit_code == 0
+            and oracle_pass is True
+            and injection_count == 0
+            and trace_completeness == "COMPLETE"
+            and not validator_errors)
+
+
+def compute_acceptance(run_role: str, fault_valid: bool,
+                       control_valid: bool) -> bool:
+    """Role-exclusive acceptance: each role is judged only by its own gate.
+
+    A CONTROL is accepted iff ``control_valid`` -- it can never be accepted
+    through the fault criteria, because a control whose injector (mis)fired
+    is an invalid control, not a fault run.  Any other role (FAULT) is
+    accepted iff ``fault_valid``; the control gate does not apply to it.
+
+    Recorded controller ruling (Task 4 fix loop): this supersedes the
+    brief's literal ``fault_valid or control_valid`` disjunction.
+    """
+    return control_valid if run_role == "CONTROL" else fault_valid

+# RP01 Candidate Comparison — v3.2.11
+
+**Decision status: NEEDS_USER_DECISION (unchanged). No candidate is approved.**
+
+## 1. Candidates
+
+- **A — Partial-forward replay loss.** In the `PartialAddrRangeCoverage` branch of `LSQUnit::read()` (`lsq_unit.cc:1990-2030`), suppress one target Load's `iewStage->rescheduleMemInst(load_inst)` call at **line 2016**. All other state transitions remain (stall record, `++replayGeneration`, `clearIssued`, `effAddrValid(false)`, `request->discard()`, `setRequest(nullptr)`, `return NoFault`).
+- **B — Strict-order replay loss.** Suppress the `rescheduleMemInst` call in the strictly-ordered-load branch at **line 1665** (`read()` 1657-1680). The load is not at the LQ head / commit head; it is normally rescheduled until it can execute in order.
+- **C — Violation/Snoop replay loss.** Suppress a `replayMemInst` drain at the store-completion/snoop sites (`lsq_unit.cc:1357` in `storePostSend`, `1496` in `completeStore`), or the violation-driven replay path (`checkViolations`, 550-636).
+
+## 2. Comparison
+
+| Criterion | A (partial forward) | B (strict order) | C (violation/snoop) |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| Real source hook | Yes, `lsq_unit.cc:2016` | Yes, `lsq_unit.cc:1665` | Yes, 1357/1496 |
+| Independent fault semantics | Removes one replay trigger after a partial-forward mismatch | Removes one strict-order retry | Removes a replay drain after store completion/snoop |
+| Data safety | No writeback is generated; no wrong PRF value; the load is simply not re-issued | The load remains strictly ordered and is re-checked when it reaches commit; likely recovered → MASKED | The load may be delayed until another drain event; no direct wrong value |
+| Observable propagation | Strong: stall record + no writeback + no commit; the fault is visible in the IQ/MemDep state | Weak: the later commit-time retry masks the skipped call; little observable effect | Medium: delay/stall; overlaps the violation path of MD04 |
+| Distinct from MD04 | Yes (replay trigger, not violation report) | Yes | **No** — C touches the same violation/replay area as MD04 |
+| Workload fit | `replay_dependency` produces real partial-forward replays (5 observed) | The workload has no dedicated strictly-ordered load; a new workload is needed | Needs a violation/snoop-triggering workload (MD04 territory) |
+| Unrecoverable HANG risk | **High** — the load is never pushed to `instsToReplay`; the store-completion `replayMemInst` has nothing to wake; ROB stalls | Low — commit-time re-check recovers it | Medium-high — depends on another drain event |
+| Diagnostic value | High: a clean "missing replay trigger" signature | Low: likely indistinguishable from normal timing | Low: confounded with MD04 |
+
+## 3. Recommendation
+
+**Recommend A**, because it is the only candidate that (a) has a real, unambiguous source hook; (b) is semantically distinct from MD04; (c) produces a strong, diagnosable signature (stall/no writeback/missing `instsToReplay` membership); and (d) fits the existing `replay_dependency` workload, which has already produced five real `partial_forward_mismatch` replays.
+
+The recommendation is **conditional** because A's predicted terminal is HANG, not SDC. The design must not claim a data-corruption result it cannot produce; it must be sold as a **replay-mechanism availability** fault whose expected terminal is TIMEOUT/HANG unless a squash intervenes.
+
+## 4. Conditions (all required before implementation)
+
+1. Researcher approval of definition A (this document does not change `DecisionStatus`).
+2. Hook only the call at `lsq_unit.cc:2016` for one ordinal-selected eligible event; do not alter the stall record, the request discard, or the return value.
+3. Prove by a unit/host test and a smoke run that no other path re-issues the skipped load (the store-completion `replayMemInst` must not recover it). If it does, the design must be revised (the fault would be MASKED).
+4. In-simulator watchdog + external timeout; on expiry log `CHAOS_RP01_WATCHDOG_EXPIRED`, save the partial trace, exit non-zero through the registered `m5_fail instruction encountered` event; classify TIMEOUT/HANG, never MASKED.
+5. Count-only preflight with the five frozen seeds/ordinals proves five distinct eligible partial-forward events. If the workload cannot supply five, report and propose a workload adjustment; do not fabricate contexts.
+6. Record identity (`seq`, `pc`, `lq_entry`, `lq_gen`, `executionAttemptId`, `replayGeneration`), the pre/post `instsToReplay` state, and the skipped-call marker in the trace.
+7. Preserve all SampleRunIDs/Seeds; no formal run this round; no overwrite of historical pilots.
+8. One-shot semantics only (no stuck-at replay removal), to bound the leak to one LQ entry.
+
+## 5. Why not B or C
+
+- **B** is likely self-healing at commit (the strict-order load is re-checked when it reaches the head), so a skipped reschedule may be unobservable — a weak fault with little diagnostic value.
+- **C** overlaps MD04's violation mechanism and needs a violation/snoop workload; it does not add an independent model.
+
+## 6. Gate
+
+**IMPLEMENTABLE_WITH_CONDITIONS** (conditions above). Pending researcher approval; `DecisionStatus` remains `NEEDS_USER_DECISION`.

+# RP01 Five Dynamic Contexts — v3.2.11 Design
+
+**Status: design only. No RP01 fault pilot was launched. The five contexts must be re-proven by a clean count-only preflight before any pilot.**
+
+## 1. Frozen identities (unchanged)
+
+| Pilot | SampleRunID | Seed | EventOrdinal |
+|---|---|---:|---:|
+| R01 | RP01-F6-W7-S01-R01 | 3135797961 | 1 |
+| R02 | RP01-F6-W7-S01-R02 | 226489823 | 2 |
+| R03 | RP01-F6-W7-S01-R03 | 1735144973 | 3 |
+| R04 | RP01-F6-W7-S01-R04 | 2455796178 | 4 |
+| R05 | RP01-F6-W7-S01-R05 | 3984724524 | 5 |
+
+## 2. Candidate contexts from the existing replay probe
+
+The existing `pilots_v3_2_2/REPLAY-RW02` run (workload `replay_dependency`, seed 3135797961, FW02 fault-on, `--prop_replay_all`) recorded exactly five `partial_forward_mismatch` replay events. They are listed here in **ordinal order** (the order they occur), not selected by outcome:
+
+| Ordinal | dyn_inst_seq | PC | Tick | LQ entry | Entry gen | Pre→post replay gen | Final result (recorded, not used for selection) |
+|---:|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
+| 1 | 69296 | 0x4015fc | 31072965 | 9200 | 9690 | 0→1 | COMMITTED |
+| 2 | 70968 | 0x42a6d8 | 35427700 | 9348 | 9912 | 0→1 | SQUASHED |
+| 3 | 70969 | 0x42a6dc | 35428085 | 9349 | 9913 | 0→1 | SQUASHED |
+| 4 | 71973 | 0x422a58 | 36564990 | 9426 | 10046 | 0→1 | COMMITTED |
+| 5 | 72305 | 0x44e2ec | 37424310 | 9466 | 10093 | 0→1 | COMMITTED |
+
+The five tuples `(seq, pc, lq_entry, entry_generation)` are all distinct. The probe's own note states the replays are real partial-forward reschedules observed under a fault run and are not claimed to be caused by the injected bit flip — so they are evidence of the **event stream**, not of RP01 causality.
+
+## 3. Proposed mapping and required preflight
+
+The mapping R01–R05 to the five observed events is a **design hypothesis**, because the probe used one seed (R01's) under a different fault. Before any RP01 pilot:
+
+1. Run a clean **count-only** probe for each of the five seeds (no injection, no fault), with the `CHAOSRP01` selector in count-only mode (to be implemented after approval) or an equivalent read-only replay observer.
+2. For each seed, record the first five eligible partial-forward events (ordinal 1–5) with `(seq, pc, tick, lq_entry, entry_generation, pre/post replay_generation)`.
+3. Require five **distinct** contexts per the table's dimensions (store/load address overlap, store coverage, load size, load PC, store PC, dependency, pre/post replay state).
+4. If a seed cannot supply five distinct events, report `WORKLOAD_INSUFFICIENT_FOR_FIVE_CONTEXTS` and propose the workload adjustment in `RP01_Workload_Verification_v3.2.11.md` §4. Do not reuse one event five times and do not select events by whether they later produce SDC or a consumer.
+5. Preserve the SampleRunIDs and Seeds above; no formal fault pilot until the researcher approves the definition and the count-only proof is attached.
+
+## 4. What a valid context must record
+
+- Target Load: `dyn_inst_seq`, PC, `lq_entry_id`, `entry_generation`, `execution_attempt_id`, `replay_generation` (pre and post).
+- Target Store: `stallingStoreIsn` / `stallingLoadIdx`, store PC, store size, coverage kind (`PartialAddrRangeCoverage`), overlapping byte range.
+- Pre-replay state: issued / `canIssue` cleared / `instsToReplay` membership.
+- Expected fault action: the single skipped `rescheduleMemInst` at `lsq_unit.cc:2016` for this ordinal.
+- Expected terminal: HANG/TIMEOUT unless a squash intervenes (then SQUASHED). No SDC claim is made for this model.
+
+## 5. Current status
+
+- Five distinct event tuples are **observed** in an existing probe.
+- Five distinct contexts under the five frozen seeds are **not yet proven**.
+- No pilot was run. The design remains `NEEDS_USER_DECISION`.

+# RP01 Final Design Gate — v3.2.11
+
+**DecisionStatus: NEEDS_USER_DECISION (unchanged). No C++ implementation in this round.**
+**Gate verdict: IMPLEMENTABLE_WITH_CONDITIONS.**
+
+## 1. Fault definition (proposed, pending approval)
+
+Skip **one** ordinal-selected `iewStage->rescheduleMemInst(load_inst)` call in the `PartialAddrRangeCoverage` branch of `LSQUnit::read()` for the target Load. One-shot (F6/F0 semantics); no change to the address, size, data, or any other state transition. The fault removes a replay trigger, not a value.
+
+## 2. Exact injection site
+
+- File: `CHAOS/gem5/src/cpu/o3/lsq_unit.cc`
+- Function: `LSQUnit::read(LSQRequest *request, ssize_t load_idx)` (1641-2075)
+- Branch: `coverage == AddrRangeCoverage::PartialAddrRangeCoverage` (1990-2030)
+- Call suppressed: **line 2016** `iewStage->rescheduleMemInst(load_inst);`
+- Eligibility: the load is the ordinal-selected eligible partial-forward event; identity `(dyn_inst_seq, lq_entry_id, entry_generation)`.
+- Everything else in the branch stays: stall record (2001-2008), `++replayGeneration` (2012), `clearIssued()` (2017), `effAddrValid(false)` (2018), `request->discard()` (2028), `load_entry.setRequest(nullptr)` (2029), `return NoFault` (2030).
+
+## 3. Normal execution path
+
+Partial store→load overlap → stall record + `++replayGeneration` → `rescheduleMemInst` → `MemDepUnit::instsToReplay` → store completion `replayMemInst` (1357/1496) → `MemDepUnit::replay` → load re-issues → full data assembly → writeback → commit.
+
+## 4. Fault execution path
+
+Partial overlap → stall record + `++replayGeneration` → **reschedule skipped** → request discarded/null → the load is never pushed to `instsToReplay` → store completion has nothing to wake → the load never writes back or commits → the ROB stalls → HANG/TIMEOUT unless an older squash clears the entry (then SQUASHED).
+
+## 5. Safety analysis
+
+| Risk | Level | Mitigation |
+|---|---|---|
+| Permanent wait | High (expected) | internal watchdog + external timeout |
+| LQ entry never freed | High | one-shot bounds it to one entry; timeout ends the run |
+| IQ resource exhaustion | Low | one-shot |
+| Duplicate scheduling/writeback | None | the call is skipped, not duplicated; no writeback is generated |
+| Wrong commit / PRF corruption | None via this mechanism | no data path is modified |
+| Simulator state corruption | Low | request discard/null is already the normal partial-branch behavior |
+
+## 6. HANG risk
+
+**This is the defining risk, not an edge case.** The expected terminal is HANG/TIMEOUT. The model must be presented as a replay-availability fault, not a data-corruption fault. A run that ends MASKED or SDC would falsify the predicted mechanism and must be investigated, not celebrated.
+
+## 7. Difference from MD04
+
+| | MD04 | RP01-A |
+|---|---|---|
+| Site | `checkViolations` return (550-636) | `read()` partial branch, line 2016 |
+| Trigger | ordering violation | partial-forward mismatch |
+| State changed | violation report/fault return | replay trigger / `instsToReplay` membership |
+| Downstream | load still executes | load never re-issues |
+| Terminal | usually MASKED | usually HANG/TIMEOUT |
+| Signature | `violation_report_suppressed=1`, data correct | skipped call, no writeback, stall |
+
+## 8. Workload verification
+
+`replay_dependency` exists, SHA256 matches `ca15390f…38f4`, oracle `59fc61c040618747` reproduced. A prior probe recorded five real `partial_forward_mismatch` replays, but under a different seed/fault; a clean count-only preflight under the five RP01 seeds is still required. The C-level same-address 8-byte store→load normally gives full coverage; the observed partial events come from the compiled dynamic stream and must be re-proven.
+
+## 9. Five dynamic contexts
+
+Identities R01–R05 preserved. A candidate mapping from the five observed replay events is in `RP01_Dynamic_Contexts_v3.2.11.md`; it is a hypothesis, not a verified context set. The count-only preflight must prove five distinct contexts under the five seeds.
+
+## 10. Expected propagation chain
+
+`injection (skip replay trigger)` → `stall record / no instsToReplay membership` → `no memory response for the attempt` → `no register writeback` → `no consumer` → `no commit` → `HANG/TIMEOUT` (or `SQUASHED` if an older squash clears the entry). **No SDC is expected.** If the design is approved, the audit classification target will be `COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN` only for a directly evidenced HANG/SQUASHED chain, never for a run that silently masks the fault.
+
+## 11. Oracle design
+
+- Normal completion (if the fault is recovered): application checksum must equal `59fc61c040618747`; a mismatch would indicate an unexpected data path and must be investigated as a design error, not claimed as RP01 SDC.
+- HANG/TIMEOUT: no checksum; the platform must classify TIMEOUT/HANG, save the partial trace, and never report MASKED.
+- The trace must record the skipped-call marker, the pre/post `instsToReplay` state, and the watchdog line.
+
+## 12. Recommendation
+
+Implement **candidate A** only under the conditions below.
+
+## Conditions (all required)
+
+1. Researcher approval of this definition; `DecisionStatus` remains `NEEDS_USER_DECISION` until then.
+2. Hook only line 2016 for one ordinal-selected eligible event; do not alter the stall record, request discard, or return value.
+3. Prove (host unit test + smoke) that no other path re-issues the skipped load; if the store-completion `replayMemInst` recovers it, revise the design.
+4. Internal watchdog + external timeout; partial trace retained; non-zero exit through the registered `m5_fail instruction encountered` event; classification TIMEOUT/HANG, never MASKED.
+5. Clean count-only preflight under the five frozen seeds proves five distinct eligible events; if not, propose a workload adjustment (preserve the original workload/SHA/golden).
+6. Record identity (`seq`, PC, `lq_entry`, `gen`, `executionAttemptId`, `replayGeneration`), pre/post replay state, and the skipped-call marker.
+7. Preserve SampleRunIDs/Seeds; no formal run this round; no historical overwrite.
+8. One-shot semantics only.
+
+## Gate verdict
+
+**IMPLEMENTABLE_WITH_CONDITIONS** — the source hook is real and independent of MD04, but the expected terminal is HANG/TIMEOUT, the workload's five distinct contexts are not yet proven, and the fault must not be sold as SDC. The design is ready for researcher review; it is not approved for implementation.

+# RP01 Source Audit — v3.2.11
+
+**Model:** RP01 (Replay trigger loss)  **SubUnit:** Replay Logic  **Mode:** F6
+**Audited source:** `CHAOS/gem5/src/cpu/o3/` at branch `lsu-platform-v3.2.11` (source unchanged since v3.2.10 except `CHAOSLQ04.cc`/`configs/se/lsu_proxy.py`).
+**No C++ hook was implemented in this round.**
+
+## 1. Exact source map (re-verified with `grep -n`/`sed -n`)
+
+| Component | File | Class / function | Lines |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| Load issue + forwarding loop | `lsq_unit.cc` | `LSQUnit::read` | **1641-2075** |
+| Request installed on the LQ entry | `lsq_unit.cc` | `load_entry.setRequest(request)` | 1647 |
+| Strictly-ordered replay branch | `lsq_unit.cc` | `read()` | **1657-1680** |
+| Strict-order `rescheduleMemInst` call | `lsq_unit.cc` | `read()` | **1665** |
+| Partial-coverage branch | `lsq_unit.cc` | `read()` | **1990-2030** |
+| Stall record (`stalled`, `stallingStoreIsn`, `stallingLoadIdx`) | `lsq_unit.cc` | `read()` | **2001-2008** |
+| `++replayGeneration` | `lsq_unit.cc` | `read()` | 2012 |
+| **Partial-forward `rescheduleMemInst` call** | `lsq_unit.cc` | `read()` | **2016** |
+| Issued/effective-address reset | `lsq_unit.cc` | `read()` | 2017-2018 |
+| Request discard + null | `lsq_unit.cc` | `read()` | 2028-2029 |
+| IEW reschedule entry | `iew.cc` | `IEW::rescheduleMemInst` | **562-566** |
+| IQ reschedule | `inst_queue.cc` | `InstructionQueue::rescheduleMemInst` | **1322-1333** |
+| MemDep queue push | `mem_dep_unit.cc` | `MemDepUnit::reschedule` | **380-384** |
+| MemDep replay drain | `mem_dep_unit.cc` | `MemDepUnit::replay` | **386-** |
+| Store-completion replay (storePostSend) | `lsq_unit.cc` | `LSQUnit::storePostSend` | **1347-1357** (call 1357) |
+| Store-completion replay (completeStore) | `lsq_unit.cc` | `LSQUnit::completeStore` | **1489-1496** (call 1496) |
+| Replay identity | `dyn_inst.hh` | `DynInst::executionAttemptId`, `replayGeneration` | **353-355** |
+| Stalling load index | `lsq_unit.hh` | `LSQUnit::stallingLoadIdx` | **565** |
+| LQ entry generation | `lsq_unit.hh` | `LSQEntry` / `lqEntryGeneration` | around 95-147 |
+
+## 2. Normal replay path (Question A)
+
+1. A Load executes `LSQUnit::read()` and scans older Stores for forwarding.
+2. `AddrRangeCoverage` is computed for each candidate Store. A **partial** overlap (`PartialAddrRangeCoverage`, `lsq_unit.cc:1990`) means the Store covers only some of the Load's bytes; the remaining bytes must come from memory after the Store is written.
+3. The Load is recorded as the stalling load (`stalled=true`, `stallingStoreIsn`, `stallingLoadIdx`, lines 2001-2008), its `replayGeneration` is incremented (2012), and `iewStage->rescheduleMemInst(load_inst)` (2016) pushes it into `InstructionQueue` → `MemDepUnit::reschedule` → `instsToReplay`.
+4. The Load's request is discarded and the LQ entry's request pointer is cleared (2028-2029); no writeback event is generated. `read()` returns `NoFault`.
+5. When the stalling Store completes, `storePostSend()`/`completeStore()` call `iewStage->replayMemInst(loadQueue[stallingLoadIdx].instruction())` (1357/1496), which calls `MemDepUnit::replay()` to move `instsToReplay` entries to the ready list. The Load re-issues, re-executes, and now assembles the full data (forwarded bytes + memory bytes), writes back, and commits.
+
+**State that decides re-execution:** `stalled`, `stallingLoadIdx`, `stallingStoreIsn`, membership in `instsToReplay`, `canIssue` (cleared by `clearIssued()`), `effAddrValid`, `executionAttemptId`, and `replayGeneration`.
+
+## 3. What happens if one `rescheduleMemInst` call is skipped (Question B)
+
+The proposed fault removes **only** the call at `lsq_unit.cc:2016` for one ordinal-selected partial-forward event; everything else (stall record, `++replayGeneration`, `clearIssued()`, `effAddrValid(false)`, `request->discard()`, `setRequest(nullptr)`, `return NoFault`) is unchanged.
+
+1. **Does the Load stay in the Issue Queue?** Yes. `clearIssued()` clears `canIssue`; the instruction remains in the IQ's issued list.
+2. **Is the wait state preserved?** Yes. `stalled`, `stallingStoreIsn`, `stallingLoadIdx` remain set; the LQ entry remains allocated with `request()==nullptr`.
+3. **Can it become permanently unreschedulable?** Yes, in the normal case. `MemDepUnit::replay()` (386-) drains `instsToReplay`; the skipped Load was never pushed because `MemDepUnit::reschedule` (380-384) is called only from the skipped path. The store-completion `replayMemInst` therefore has nothing to wake for this Load. No other path re-issues this specific dynamic Load.
+4. **Can it use incomplete data?** No. The partial branch generates no writeback event and returns before data assembly; the Load is not executed.
+5. **Can it write wrong data to the PRF?** No direct path — there is no writeback for this attempt.
+6. **Can another replay mechanism recover it?** `replayMemInst` only drains `instsToReplay`; `wakeDependents` does not re-issue an unexecuted memory instruction. A branch misprediction squash can clear the entry, but that is a different mechanism and not guaranteed.
+7. **Can it be squashed?** Yes, if an older instruction squashes. The squash loop clears `stalled` and pops the LQ entry; the dynamic Load then disappears without committing. That is a recovery-ish outcome, not a propagation of the fault.
+8. **Can it hang?** Yes — and this is the predicted deterministic outcome. The Load never completes; the ROB head (or a dependent instruction) waits indefinitely; the pipeline drains and the simulation deadlocks. The expected terminal is **HANG/TIMEOUT**, not SDC. This is the central safety finding.
+
+## 4. Normal vs fault execution
+
+| Phase | Normal | Fault (skip one call) |
+|---|---|---|
+| Partial overlap detected | stall record + replayGeneration | same |
+| IQ/MemDep reschedule | `instsToReplay` push | **missing** |
+| Request | discarded, entry null | same |
+| Store completion | `replayMemInst` wakes the Load | nothing to wake |
+| Load re-execution | full data assembly | never happens |
+| Writeback / commit | correct value, commit | none; ROB stalls |
+| Terminal | MASKED or SDC depending on data | **HANG** (unless squashed) |
+
+## 5. Safety analysis (Question D)
+
+| Risk | Assessment |
+|---|---|
+| Permanent wait | **High** — the most likely outcome |
+| LQ entry never released | **High** — the entry cannot commit; one-shot fault bounds the leak to one entry |
+| IQ resource exhaustion | Low — one-shot, one entry |
+| Duplicate scheduling | None — the call is skipped, not duplicated |
+| Duplicate writeback | None — no writeback for the skipped attempt |
+| Wrong commit | None via this mechanism |
+| Simulator state corruption | Low — the request was already discarded and the entry nulled consistently; the stall record is stale but bounded |
+
+**Watchdog/timeout design:** an in-simulator `EventFunctionWrapper` armed at injection (or at the first eligible partial event), logging `CHAOS_RP01_WATCHDOG_EXPIRED` and exiting through gem5's registered `m5_fail instruction encountered` event with code 1 (the v3.2.11 LQ04 precedent). The external `run_pilot.py` timeout preserves the partial trace and must classify the result as `TIMEOUT`/`HANG`, never `MASKED`. The last valid trace event must be retained (gzip closed by the exit callback).
+
+## 6. Question C — RP01 vs MD04
+
+| Dimension | MD04 | RP01 candidate A |
+|---|---|---|
+| Injection site | `LSQUnit::checkViolations` return (`lsq_unit.cc:550-636`) | `LSQUnit::read` partial branch (`2016`) |
+| Trigger | a real Store→Younger-Load ordering violation | a partial-coverage forwarding mismatch |
+| Modified state | the violation report/fault return | the replay trigger/`instsToReplay` membership |
+| Downstream behavior | Load still executes; data path unchanged | Load is never re-issued; ROB stalls |
+| Typical terminal | MASKED (or SDC if the stale data is consumed) | HANG/TIMEOUT |
+| Diagnostic signature | `violation_report_suppressed=1`, correct data | skipped `rescheduleMemInst`, no writeback, stall |
+| Safety profile | bounded, often MASKED | deadlock risk; requires watchdog |
+
+RP01 is not a re-labelled MD04: it modifies a different control state, at a different site, with a different downstream signature.

+# RP01 Workload Verification — v3.2.11
+
+**Workload:** `replay_dependency`  **Expected SHA256:** `ca15390f1a797c55074703dc3fe10d1e07f7704794a9abca96b2761c41d838f4`
+**Expected Oracle:** `59fc61c040618747`
+
+## 1. Existence and identity
+
+| Check | Result |
+|---|---|
+| `docs/gem5-fi/lsu/implementation_v3_2/workloads/replay_dependency` exists | **yes** (ELF) |
+| SHA256 | **matches** `ca15390f…38f4` |
+| Duplicate `final_paper_audit_v3_1/workload_sources/replay_dependency` | same SHA256 |
+| `replay_dependency.golden` | `59fc61c040618747` |
+| `replay_dependency.host.out` | `checksum=0x59fc61c040618747` |
+| Golden reproducible on the host | **yes** (the golden file is the host run output) |
+
+No re-generation and no substitution was performed. The workload is the original W7 file.
+
+## 2. Source analysis (`replay_dependency.c`)
+
+- `static volatile uint64_t a[1024]`, initialized to `a[i]=i`.
+- 256 iterations: `idx=(r*37)&1023; q[idx]=r; asm volatile("" ::: "memory"); s+=p[idx];`
+- The store and the load are the **same 8-byte address** (`q[idx]` / `p[idx]`, both `uint64_t *`), separated by a compiler memory barrier.
+- The checksum is `s ^ fnv1a(a,4096)`; the loaded value directly feeds `s`, so a downstream consumer is observable through the application checksum.
+
+**Important caveat:** at the C level the store and load are full-width (8-byte) same-address accesses, which normally produce **full** address-range coverage, not `PartialAddrRangeCoverage`. The five partial-forward replay events observed in the existing probe (below) therefore come from other dynamic access patterns in the compiled binary (compiler-generated sequences, overlapping adjacent iterations, or split accesses), not from the literal `q[idx]=r; s+=p[idx]` pair. Whether the compiled workload deterministically produces five distinct partial-forward events under each RP01 seed must be established by a **clean count-only preflight**. The source alone does not prove it.
+
+## 3. Existing replay evidence (read-only, not a substitute for the count-only preflight)
+
+`pilots_v3_2_2/REPLAY-RW02/replay_pilot_summary.json` (workload `replay_dependency`, seed 3135797961, FW02 fault-on, `--prop_replay_all`):
+
+- `replay_count = 5`
+- Reason for all five: `partial_forward_mismatch`
+- Distinct `(seq, pc, tick, lq_entry, entry_generation)` tuples; 3 `COMMITTED`, 2 `SQUASHED`
+- The summary explicitly notes: *“replays are real partial_forward_mismatch reschedules of replay_dependency; they are observed under a fault run and are not claimed to be caused by the injected bit flip.”*
+
+This proves the workload **can** produce at least five real partial-forward replays, but it does **not** prove that the five frozen RP01 seeds/ordinals produce five distinct eligible events. The binary and seed differ from the RP01 plan.
+
+## 4. Requirements still open
+
+1. A clean no-fault count-only run (no injection) with the five frozen seeds and ordinal 1–5 must list the first five eligible partial-forward events per seed and prove five distinct dynamic contexts. This is a **future, approval-gated** step; it was not executed in this round.
+2. If a seed yields fewer than five distinct events, report `WORKLOAD_INSUFFICIENT_FOR_FIVE_CONTEXTS` and propose a workload adjustment: add a deliberate partial overlap (e.g., two 4-byte stores covering one 8-byte load, or an 8-byte store and an 8-byte load offset by 4 bytes) in a **new** workload, preserve `replay_dependency` and its SHA/golden, record the new SHA256 and re-validate the golden oracle. Do not silently replace the original.
+
+## 5. Verdict
+
+- File and SHA: **verified**.
+- Oracle: **verified** (`59fc61c040618747`).
+- Partial-forward replay capability: **observed** (5 events in a prior probe), but not yet proven under the five RP01 seeds.
+- Workload status for RP01: **VERIFIED_WITH_PREFLIGHT_REQUIRED**.

+# LSU v3.2.11 — LQ04 Closure, 11-Model Propagation Audit, RP01 Design Implementation Plan
+
+> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
+
+**Goal:** Close the four LQ04 leftovers, independently audit the fault-impact propagation chain of all 11 implemented LSU models from raw traces, and complete the RP01 source audit plus a gated final fault-injection design, then publish the v3.2.11 Excel, model matrix and unified ZIP.
+
+**Architecture:** Three independent evidence tracks share one platform: (A) LQ04 semantics/tooling fixes that separate structural chain completeness from fault-effect propagation; (B) a streaming audit tool that re-derives each pilot's seven stages from `trace_v31.jsonl` plus `pilot_meta.json` and emits per-model reports and one matrix; (C) a source-only RP01 study of `LSQUnit::read()` partial-coverage replay and its three candidate fault mechanisms. No new formal experiment is started; the only runtime execution permitted is one LQ04 watchdog smoke after the C++ fix.
+
+**Tech Stack:** C++17 (vendored gem5 v25.1.0.1, SCons), Python 3.11 (gem5 stdlib config, pilot platform, pytest, openpyxl), AArch64 host `gcc -O2 -static`.

+## Evidence Map (verified at plan time)
+

+|---|---|---|---|

+| SQ01 | `pilots_v3_2_3/SQ01-R01..R05`, `pilots_v3_2_4/SQ01-R01..R05` | `LSU_v3.2.3_Propagation_Closure_Package.zip` (worktree `lsu-platform-v3.2`) | in-tree dirs keep `trace_v31.jsonl.sha256` only |
+| DA04 | `pilots_v3_2_6/DA04-R04,R05` (authoritative), `pilots_v3_2_4/DA04-R01..R05` | in-tree | 20–34 MB each |
+| LP01 | `pilots_v3_2_6/LP01-R01..R05` | in-tree | 43 MB each |
+| MD04 | `pilots_v3_2_6/MD04-R01..R05` | in-tree | 26–27 MB each; v3.2.7 dirs are meta-only |
+| FW05 | `pilots_v3_2_7/FW05-R01..R05` | `LSU_v3.2.7_MD04_Config_Freeze_FW05_Implementation_Package.zip` (v3.2.10 worktree root) | 39 MB JSONL each in the zip |
+| SQ03 | `pilots_v3_2_8/SQ03-R01..R05` | `LSU_v3.2.8_FW05_ConfigFreeze_SQ03_Pilot_Package.zip` (`/tmp` and `lsu-platform-v3.2` root) | 196 MB each |
+| SP05 | `LSU_v3.2.9_..._Package.zip` → `A_SP05/pilots/SP05-R01..R05` | same zip | 92 MB each; in-tree only `SP05_v329_EVIDENCE.csv` |
+| LQ04 | `pilots_v3_2_10/LQ04-R01..R05` + `LQ04-CONTROL-R01..R05` | in-tree | 17 MB each |
+
+## Review Focus
+
+1. **Historical artifacts must not be touched.** A report or matrix step that writes into `pilots_v3_2_*` invalidates the evidence chain. Task 9 verifies `sha256sum` before/after extraction and Task 21 asserts `git status --short` has no `pilots_v3_2_*` entry.
+2. **Time adjacency is not causality.** The audit tool must only create a chain edge from an explicit `ptag`, `dyn_inst_seq`, or data-hash link. Task 8's synthetic test has two adjacent events with no link and must return `PARTIAL`/`UNRESOLVED`, never `COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN`.
+3. **Control runs are not failed fault runs.** Task 4 pins `run_role=CONTROL`, `control_valid=true`, `accepted=true` for an oracle-passing zero-injection run.
+4. **Watchdog must exit cleanly and never be MASKED.** Task 5 asserts non-zero exit, the `CHAOS_LQ04_WATCHDOG_EXPIRED` line, absence of `NotImplementedError`, retained trace events, and `classify` returning `TIMEOUT`/`HANG` rather than `MASKED`.
+5. **Mixed binaries must not be aggregated.** Task 8's `audit_pilot` refuses to compare a pilot's recorded binary SHA with a different audit binary; Task 21 emits per-binary splits in the matrix instead of a merged verdict.
+

+  - `fault_effect_coverage.classify(model: str, events: list[dict], injection: dict | None, terminal: str) -> dict`
+  - Returns `{"fault_effect_propagation_coverage": "COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN"|"PARTIAL"|"LIFECYCLE_ONLY"|"UNRESOLVED", "fault_effect_evidence": {...}, "fault_effect_reason": str}`.
+- Consumes: trace JSONL events produced by `prop_trace_to_v31.py`.

+- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**
+
+Assertions (synthetic event lists, no real trace needed):
+```python

+assert classify("LQ04", lq04_correct_events, lq04_injection, "MASKED")[
+    "fault_effect_propagation_coverage"] == "LIFECYCLE_ONLY"
+# A consumer edge whose input hash differs from the source hash -> complete causal chain
+assert classify("MD04", md04_diverged_events, md04_injection, "MASKED")[
+    "fault_effect_propagation_coverage"] == "COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN"
+# Injection plus an unrelated adjacent event (no ptag/seq/hash link) -> never complete
+assert classify("FW02", adjacent_unlinked_events, fw02_injection, "UNRESOLVED")[
+    "fault_effect_propagation_coverage"] in ("PARTIAL", "UNRESOLVED")
+# No injection event -> UNRESOLVED, not COMPLETE
+assert classify("SQ01", events_without_injection, None, "UNRESOLVED")[
+    "fault_effect_propagation_coverage"] == "UNRESOLVED"
+```
+
+- [ ] **Step 2: Run and see it fail**
+
+Run: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_fault_effect_coverage.py -v`
+Expected: FAIL/collection error (module missing).
+
+- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the classifier**
+
+Rules: only events whose `ptag == injection.ptag` or `dyn_inst_seq == injection.dyn_inst_seq` form the chain (`terminal_rules.chain_events` may be reused). `value_diverged` = a `register_writeback`/`consumer_commit` with `data_corruption=1`, `consumer_input_matches_source=0`, or `consumer_input_fnv != source_value_hash`. `control_diverged` = a model marker from `EFFECT_MARKERS[model]` present with a downstream linked event. Return `COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN` only when a divergence plus a downstream commit/terminal explanation exists; `PARTIAL` when divergence/marker exists without the downstream link; `LIFECYCLE_ONLY` when the injection changed state but nothing diverged; `UNRESOLVED` when there is no injection or the trace is incomplete. LQ04's marker list is empty by design (its effect is the entry-lifetime window), so the LQ04 pilots must grade `LIFECYCLE_ONLY`.
+
+- [ ] **Step 4: Integrate into the platform fields**
+
+In `trace_completeness.py`, keep `propagation_coverage` unchanged and add `structural_chain_completeness` with the same value. In `run_pilot.py`'s `classification` dict, add `structural_chain_completeness` and the three `fault_effect_*` fields from `classify(...)`. Do not rewrite historical `pilot_meta.json`.

+- [ ] **Step 1: Confirm the root cause**
+
+Read `CHAOS/gem5/src/python/gem5/simulate/exit_event.py:131`: unknown exit strings raise `NotImplementedError`. The only supported strings are the enumerated ones (`m5_fail instruction encountered` maps to `ExitEvent.FAIL`, code carried by `exitSimLoop`).
+
+- [ ] **Step 2: Change the exit call**
+
+Replace `exitSimLoop("lq04 watchdog expired", 1);` with:
+```cpp
+// gem5's stdlib Simulator maps exit strings to ExitEvent values and raises
+// NotImplementedError for unknown strings. Use the registered FAIL event;
+// the experimental reason is the CHAOS_LQ04_WATCHDOG_EXPIRED log line above.
+exitSimLoop("m5_fail instruction encountered", 1);
+```
+No other watchdog behavior changes: the log line and stdout line stay, and the last trace event stays because exit callbacks close the gzip stream.

+### Task 8: Build the propagation-audit tool (B2/B6/B7)
+
+**Files:**
+- Create: `tools/lsu_propagation_audit.py`
+- Create: `tests/test_lsu_propagation_audit.py`
+
+**Interfaces:**
+- Produces:
+  - `audit_pilot(pilot_dir: Path, model: str, binary_sha: str | None = None) -> dict` returning `{identity, stages, evidence, terminal, oracle, missing, classification}`.
+  - `audit_model(model: str, pilot_dirs: list[Path]) -> dict` returning the matrix row fields.
+  - `write_matrix(rows: list[dict], out: Path) -> None`.
+- Stage fields: `injection_verified`, `activation_verified`, `early_anomaly_verified`, `intermediate_propagation_verified`, `register_writeback_verified`, `consumer_verified`, `commit_verified`, `terminal_verified`, `oracle_verified` (each `yes|no|n/a|missing`).
+- Evidence fields: `injection_seq`, `pc`, `tick`, `event_ordinal`, earliest anomaly event id/detail, writeback phys reg/hash, consumer first event/PC/input hash, commit seq, terminal/reason, oracle expected/actual.
+
+- [x] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**
+
+Use synthetic JSONL/trace fixtures:
+```python
+# linked chain with matching hashes -> all stages yes, classification COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN only if divergence

+### Tasks 10–20: Per-model seven-stage audits (B5/B6)
+
+One task and one commit per model, in this order: A01, A03, FW02, SQ01, DA04, LP01, MD04, FW05, SQ03, SP05, LQ04. Each produces:
+`docs/gem5-fi/lsu/implementation_v3_2/audit_v3_2_11/<MODEL>_Propagation_Audit_v3.2.11.md`
+
+Every report uses the same template:
+1. Identity/provenance table (SampleRunID, seed, ordinal, binary SHA, workload SHA, oracle) — one row per pilot.
+2. Seven-stage table — one row per pilot (`injection`, `activation`, `early anomaly`, `intermediate`, `writeback`, `consumer`, `commit`, `terminal`, `oracle`).
+3. Quoted primary evidence (`file:line`, event id, hashes).
+4. Terminal causality (observed terminal vs fault causality, separately).
+5. Oracle comparison.
+6. Missing evidence list.
+7. `FinalClassification` (`COMPLETE_CAUSAL_CHAIN`/`LIFECYCLE_ONLY`/`PARTIAL`/`UNRESOLVED`).
+8. Supplementary-pilot recommendation with the B8 justification fields (or `none`).
+
+Model-specific expected paths to check (do not copy this list into a report verbatim; derive each from the model's frozen definition in Excel sheet 4):
+
+- **A01**: address-based fault → wrong EA → request addressee → response → writeback value sourced from the wrong EA → consumer hash → commit/squash. Check the injected EA transform (`EA ^ 0x8`), and whether the response data corresponds to the corrupted address. Historical verdict: R03 SQUASHED.
+- **A03**: F5 repeating EA-bit3 fault → per-event activation → crash evidence. Check that a CRASH is not accepted without the stderr page-table-fault address matching the trace's last injection; distinguish `ObservedTerminal` from `FaultCausality`.
+- **FW02**: suppressed forward decision → which source served the load → response/writeback hash vs the true store → consumer. Check the `forwarding_decision` suppression event and whether the load's data came from the correct or wrong source.
+- **SQ01**: store data byte0 corruption → `store_writeback` → `store_mem_write` → later load `memory_response` → writeback → consumer. If no load ever reads the lane, the honest class is `LIFECYCLE_ONLY`/`PARTIAL`, not complete.
+- **DA04**: byte-enable `0xF→0xE` → `store_writeback` → `store_mem_write` → later load response → writeback → consumer. Re-derive the claimed 1,009/7,714 matched consumer edges from the v3.2.6 R04/R05 traces; verify zero mismatches independently.
+- **LP01**: response byte0 corruption → `memory_response` → `register_writeback` → consumer. Re-derive the claimed mismatch diagnostics 45/0/3/0/3 and check each mismatch has a real chain link.
+- **MD04**: violation-check suppression → load execution decision → memory/load path → writeback → consumer → commit/squash. Check the cross-attempt evidence for R03 (259 consumers) has real `executionAttemptId` links; R01/R04/R05 SQUASHED.
+- **FW05**: wrong forwarding source → `forwarding_decision` alt-src → load data (alt hash) → writeback → consumer. Check the alt-seq/alt-fnv fields and whether the consumer received the alt data; verify the claimed SDC checksum `549c…` against the trace and oracle.
+- **SQ03**: premature writeback permission → `store_writeback` → `store_mem_write` → `completeStore`; check the claimed permission/cache-update counts (140910/198275) and whether any later load consumes the lane. Historical verdict: `PROPAGATION_VERIFIED=0`.
+- **SP05**: suppressed `completeStore` → `_completed`/`storesToWB` unchanged → entry lifetime/drain; check `completed_before/after`, `stores_to_wb_before/after`, and whether any consumer exists. Historical verdict: MASKED, 0 consumers.
+- **LQ04**: entry `valid/request` cleared → response via `senderState` → correct writeback → consumers carry correct payload → commit → oracle PASS. Expected: `LIFECYCLE_ONLY`, `PROPAGATION_VERIFIED=0`.
+
+Each task's steps:
+- [ ] Run `python3 tools/lsu_propagation_audit.py --model <M> --pilot-dir <dirs...> --json <tmp>` and quote the real output.
+- [ ] Open the raw trace at the cited lines and verify the extracted evidence (event id, hash, seq) by hand.
+- [ ] Write the report with the template; no `PASS`/`COMPLETE` without evidence.
+- [ ] Commit with `[LSU][MDSPEC][V3.2.11][AUDIT] audit <M> propagation from raw trace evidence`.
+

+### Task 23: RP01 source audit — normal replay path and failure modes (C1/C2 A/B/D)
+
+**Files:**
+- Create: `docs/gem5-fi/lsu/implementation_v3_2/rp01_design_v3_2_11/RP01_Source_Audit_v3.2.11.md`
+
+**Interfaces:**
+- Consumes: `CHAOS/gem5/src/cpu/o3/lsq_unit.cc`, `iew.cc`, `inst_queue.cc`, `mem_dep_unit.cc`, `dyn_inst.hh`.
+- Produces: file/class/function/line tables, call graph, member variables, normal path, and answers A/B/D.
+
+- [ ] **Step 1: Record the verified source locations**
+
+At plan time: `LSQUnit::read` `lsq_unit.cc:1641-2075`; partial-coverage branch `1990-2030`; `rescheduleMemInst` call `2016`; strictly-ordered branch `1657-1680` (call `1665`); stall record `2001-2008`; `IEW::rescheduleMemInst` `iew.cc:562-566`; `InstructionQueue::rescheduleMemInst` `inst_queue.cc:1322-1333`; `MemDepUnit::reschedule` `mem_dep_unit.cc:380-384`; `MemDepUnit::replay` `386-...`; `DynInst::executionAttemptId/replayGeneration` `dyn_inst.hh:353-355`; `LSQUnit::stallingLoadIdx` `lsq_unit.hh:565`. Re-verify each with `grep -n` and quote the line.
+
+- [ ] **Step 2: Answer Question A (why replay happens)**
+
+Trace: partial store→load overlap → `PartialAddrRangeCoverage` → the load's stall record (`stalled`, `stallingStoreIsn`, `stallingLoadIdx`) → `++replayGeneration` → `rescheduleMemInst` → `instsToReplay` → `MemDepUnit::replay` on the store-completion path → IQ wake → load re-executes → full forwarding → commit.
+
+- [ ] **Step 3: Answer Question B (skip one reschedule)**
+
+Reason from the bodies, not names: whether the load stays in the IQ (it does; `clearIssued()` only clears canIssue), whether `stalled`/`stallingLoadIdx` remain, whether `MemDepUnit::replay()` can recover an inst that was never pushed to `instsToReplay` (it cannot), whether the request is already discarded (`request->discard()`, `setRequest(nullptr)`), and whether any other mechanism re-issues it. Conclude with the concrete predicted terminal (HANG vs replayed vs wrong data) and cite the lines.
+
+- [ ] **Step 4: Answer Question D (safety)**
+
+Enumerate: permanent wait, LQ entry never freed, IQ resource exhaustion, duplicate scheduling, duplicate writeback, wrong commit, internal state corruption. Propose the internal watchdog and external timeout, and the rule that a timeout saves partial trace and is never MASKED.
+
+- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**
+
+```bash

+git commit -m "[LSU][MDSPEC][V3.2.11][RP01] audit the replay source path and the skipped-reschedule failure modes"
+```
